\mu^{i,j,+}_{\varphi}(\mathrm{d}\theta):=\max\{M_{\varphi}(\theta)^{i,j},0\}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\ \ \text{and}\ \ \mu^{i,j,-}_{\varphi}(\mathrm{d}\theta):=\max\{-M_{\varphi}(\theta)^{i,j},0\}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta).

Clearly, the expressions in (
4.4
) hold. From (
4.2
) and (
4.3
), using the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality, we can show that

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big(1\wedge(\theta^{-\frac{1+\alpha}{2}})\big)\,\mu^{i,j,\pm}_{b}(\mathrm{d}\theta)<\infty\ \ \text{and}\ \ \int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big(1\wedge(\theta^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}})\big)\,\mu^{i,j,\pm}_{\sigma}(\mathrm{d}\theta)<\infty\ \ \text{for any $i,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}$.}

(4.7)

By Bernstein’s theorem (cf.
[
29
, Theorem 1.4]
), we see that
K^{i,j,\pm}_{b}
and
K^{i,j,\pm}_{\sigma}
are completely monotone functions. Furthermore,
[
13
, Lemma 2.1]
shows that (
4.7
) is equivalent to (
4.5
) and implies that
K^{i,j,\pm}_{b}\in L^{\frac{2}{1+\alpha}}(0,T;\mathbb{R})
and
K^{i,j,\pm}_{\sigma}\in L^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}(0,T;\mathbb{R})
for each
i,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
. The latter yields that
K_{b}\in L^{\frac{2}{1+\alpha}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
and
K_{\sigma}\in L^{\frac{2}{\alpha}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
.

Conversely, assume that
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
are in form of (
4.4
) for some completely monotone functions
K^{i,j,\pm}_{b},K^{i,j,\pm}_{\sigma}:(0,\infty)\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying (
4.5
). Again by Bernstein’s theorem (cf.
[
29
, Theorem 1.4]
) and
[
13
, Lemma 2.1]
, for each
i,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}
, there exist (unique) Borel measures
\mu^{i,j,\pm}_{b}
and
\mu^{i,j,\pm}_{\sigma}
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
satisfying (
4.6
) and (
4.7
). Define a Borel measure
\mu
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
by

\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta):=\big(1+\theta\big)^{-\frac{\alpha}{2}}\sum^{n}_{i,j=1}\big\{\mu^{i,j,+}_{b}(\mathrm{d}\theta)+\mu^{i,j,-}_{b}(\mathrm{d}\theta)\big\}+(1+\theta)^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}\sum^{n}_{i,j=1}\big\{\mu^{i,j,+}_{\sigma}(\mathrm{d}\theta)+\mu^{i,j,-}_{\sigma}(\mathrm{d}\theta)\big\}.

Notice that
\mu^{i,j,\pm}_{b}
and
\mu^{i,j,\pm}_{\sigma}
are absolutely continuous with respect to
\mu
. Furthermore, by (
4.7
), we see that
\mu
satisfies the integrability condition (
4.2
). Define matrix-valued Borel measurable maps
M_{b},M_{\sigma}:\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
by

M_{b}:=\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{i,j,+}_{b}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}-\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{i,j,-}_{b}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right)^{n}_{i,j=1},\ \ M_{\sigma}:=\left(\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{i,j,+}_{\sigma}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}-\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{i,j,-}_{\sigma}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}\right)^{n}_{i,j=1}.

Noting that

0\leq\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{i,j,\pm}_{b}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}(\theta)\leq\big(1+\theta)^{\frac{\alpha}{2}}\ \ \text{and}\ \ 0\leq\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{i,j,\pm}_{\sigma}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}(\theta)\leq\big(1+\theta)^{-\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}\ \ \text{for $\mu$-a.e.\ $\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}$ for any $i,j\in\{1,\dots,n\}$,}

we see that
M_{b}
and
M_{\sigma}
satisfy the integrability condition (
4.3
). By (
4.4
) and (
4.6
), we see that the representations in (
4.1
) hold. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.3
.

•

From (
4.5
), the number
\alpha\in[0,1)
is regarded as the degree of singularity of the kernels
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
at zero. Namely, the larger the number
\alpha
is, the more singular the kernels
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
can be. When
\alpha=0
, the kernel
K_{\sigma}
appearing in the stochastic integral term becomes regular in the sense that
\lim_{t\downarrow 0}|K_{\sigma}(t)|<\infty
, while the kernel
K_{b}
appearing in the Lebesgue integral term is still allowed to be singular and belongs to
L^{2}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
. The number
\alpha
can also be seen as the degree of the “heavy tail” (that is, the weakness of the integrability at
\infty
) of the vector measures
M_{b}(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)
and
M_{\sigma}(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)
in the representation (
4.1
).

•

From the last assertion of
Lemma

4.1
and
Remark

2.1
, under
Assumption

2
, we have
\|K_{b}\|_{1,\varepsilon}\leq\|K_{b}\|_{2/(1+\alpha),\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}
and
\|K_{\sigma}\|_{2,\varepsilon}\leq\|K_{\sigma}\|_{2/\alpha,\varepsilon}\varepsilon^{\frac{1-\alpha}{2}}
. The conditions that
K_{b}\in L^{3/2}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
and
K_{\sigma}\in L^{6}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
assumed in the last assertion of
Proposition

3.1
are satisfied if
Assumption

2
holds with
\alpha=\frac{1}{3}
.

•

Important examples of completely monotone functions can be found in
[
13
, Example 2.3]
. For example, consider the fractional kernels in form of

K_{b}(t)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\beta_{b})}t^{\beta_{b}-1}I_{n\times n},\ \ K_{\sigma}(t)=\frac{1}{\Gamma(\beta_{\sigma})}t^{\beta_{\sigma}-1}I_{n\times n},

for some constants
\beta_{b}\in(0,1)
and
\beta_{\sigma}\in(\frac{1}{2},1)
. Here,
\Gamma(\beta):=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\theta^{\beta-1}e^{-\theta}\,\mathrm{d}\theta
,
\beta>0
, denotes the Gamma function. These kernels satisfy
Assumption

2
for any
(1-2\beta_{b})\vee(2-2\beta_{\sigma})<\alpha<1
. Indeed, we can take

\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)=\theta^{-\gamma}\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{(0,\infty)}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}\theta,\ \ M_{b}(\theta)=\frac{\theta^{\gamma-\beta_{b}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{b})\Gamma(1-\beta_{b})}I_{n\times n},\ \ M_{\sigma}(\theta)=\frac{\theta^{\gamma-\beta_{\sigma}}}{\Gamma(\beta_{\sigma})\Gamma(1-\beta_{\sigma})}I_{n\times n},

where
\gamma
is an arbitrary constant such that
\frac{1}{2}<\gamma<(\alpha+2\beta_{b}-\frac{1}{2})\wedge(\alpha+2\beta_{\sigma}-\frac{3}{2})\wedge 1
.

•

Suppose that
(K_{b}^{1},K_{\sigma}^{1})
and
(K_{b}^{2},K_{\sigma}^{2})
satisfy
Assumption

2
with
(\mu^{i},M_{b}^{i},M_{\sigma}^{i},\alpha^{i})
,
i=1,2
, as respective tuples. Then, for any constants
c_{b}^{1},c_{b}^{1},c_{\sigma}^{1},c_{\sigma}^{2}\in\mathbb{R}
, the pair
(K_{b},K_{\sigma})=(c_{b}^{1}K_{b}^{1}+c_{b}^{2}K_{b}^{2},c_{\sigma}^{1}K_{\sigma}^{1}+c_{\sigma}^{2}K_{\sigma}^{2})
satisfies
Assumption

2
with

\mu=\mu^{1}+\mu^{2},\ \ M_{b}=c_{b}^{1}M_{b}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{1}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}+c_{b}^{2}M_{b}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{2}}{\mathrm{d}\mu},\ \ M_{\sigma}=c_{\sigma}^{1}M_{\sigma}^{1}\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{1}}{\mathrm{d}\mu}+c_{\sigma}^{2}M_{\sigma}^{2}\frac{\mathrm{d}\mu^{2}}{\mathrm{d}\mu},\ \ \alpha=\alpha^{1}\vee\alpha^{2}.

4.1
Lifting the variational SVEs

Suppose that
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

2
hold. Fix
\hat{u},v\in\mathcal{U}
,
\tau\in[0,T)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,T-\tau]
. We use the same notations as in
Section

3
.

Following
[
13
]
, we introduce “lifts” of the variational SVEs (
3.4
) and (
3.6
) for
X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
and
X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
, respectively. For each
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
, consider the following SDEs on
\mathbb{R}^{n}
parametrized by
\theta
:

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)=-\theta Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t+M_{b}(\theta)\big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}+\delta b^{v}(t)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(t)\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\\
\hskip 113.81102pt+M_{\sigma}(\theta)\big\{\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}+\delta\sigma^{v}(t)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(t)\big\}\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{0}(\theta)=0,\end{cases}

(4.8)

and

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)=-\theta Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t+M_{b}(\theta)\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}+\frac{1}{2}\big\langle\hat{b}_{xx}(t)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t},X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\big\rangle\\
\hskip 199.16928pt+\Big(\delta b^{v}(t)+\delta b^{v}_{x}(t)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\Big)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(t)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\\
\hskip 62.59596pt+M_{\sigma}(\theta)\Big\{\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}+\frac{1}{2}\big\langle\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t},X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\big\rangle\\
\hskip 142.26378pt+\Big(\delta\sigma^{v}(t)+\delta\sigma^{v}_{x}(t)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\Big)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(t)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{0}(\theta)=0.\end{cases}

(4.9)

Noting (
3.1
) and
X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon},X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}\in\bigcap_{p\in[2,\infty)}C^{p}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})
, the above SDEs admit unique solutions
Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}(\theta)
and
Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}(\theta)
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)|^{p}+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)|^{p}\right]<\infty\ \ \text{for any $p\in[2,\infty)$.}

The solutions have the following expressions:

\begin{split}Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)&=\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\theta(t-s)}M_{b}(\theta)\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(s)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}+\delta b^{v}(s)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(s)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\theta(t-s)}M_{\sigma}(\theta)\Big\{\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}+\delta\sigma^{v}(s)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(s)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\end{split}

(4.10)

and

\begin{split}Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)&=\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\theta(t-s)}M_{b}(\theta)\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(s)X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}+\frac{1}{2}\big\langle\hat{b}_{xx}(s)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s},X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}\big\rangle\\
&\hskip 128.0374pt+\Big(\delta b^{v}(s)+\delta b^{v}_{x}(s)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}\Big)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(s)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\theta(t-s)}M_{\sigma}(\theta)\Big\{\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}+\frac{1}{2}\big\langle\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(s)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s},X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}\big\rangle\\
&\hskip 142.26378pt+\Big(\delta\sigma^{v}(s)+\delta\sigma^{v}_{x}(s)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}\Big)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(s)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}.\end{split}

(4.11)

By the standard stability argument, one can show that there exist (jointly) progressively measurable versions of
Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon},Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
. In the following, we consider such versions.

As investigated in
[
13
]
,
Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
and
Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
can be seen as (infinite dimensional)
lifts
of
X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
and
X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
, respectively. Indeed, the following lemma shows that
X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}
and
X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}
are represented as the integrals of
Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)
and
Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)
with respect to
\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)
. In the following, for each
\mu
-integrable function
\psi
on
\mathbb{R}_{+}
taking values in a Euclidean space, we denote

\mu[\psi]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\psi(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta).

(4.12)

Lemma 4.4
.

Suppose that
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

2
hold. Fix
\hat{u},v\in\mathcal{U}
,
\tau\in[0,T)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,T-\tau]
. Then, for any
p\in[2,\infty)
, there exists a constant
C_{p,\alpha}>0
, which does not depend on
\tau
or
\varepsilon
, such that

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)r(\theta)\Big\{\|Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}(\theta)\|_{C^{p}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}^{2}+\|Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}(\theta)\|_{C^{p}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}^{2}\Big\}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\leq C_{p,\alpha}\varepsilon^{1-\alpha}.

(4.13)

In particular,

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\|^{p}_{L^{1}(\mu)}+\|Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\|^{p}_{L^{1}(\mu)}\Big]<\infty

(4.14)

for any
p\in[2,\infty)
. Furthermore, it holds that

\mu[Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}]=X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \mu[Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}]=X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\ \ \text{a.s.\ for any $t\in[0,T]$.}

(4.15)

Proof.

Let
p\in[2,\infty)
. We first show (
4.13
). Fix
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
. Noting (
4.10
) and (
4.11
), by using
Lemma

2.2
(applying to the kernel
t\mapsto e^{-\theta t}
) and
Proposition

3.1
, we obtain

\begin{split}&\|Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}(\theta)\|_{C^{p}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}+\|Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}(\theta)\|_{C^{p}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\\
&\leq C_{p}\big\{\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{1}(0,T)}|M_{b}(\theta)|+\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{2}(0,T)}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|\big\}\big\{\|K_{b}\|_{1,\varepsilon}+\|K_{\sigma}\|_{2,\varepsilon}\big\}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+C_{p}\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{1}(0,\varepsilon)}|M_{b}(\theta)|+C_{p}\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{2}(0,\varepsilon)}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|,\end{split}

(4.16)

for some constant
C_{p}>0
, which does not depend on
\tau
or
\varepsilon
. Noting
Lemma

4.1
and
Remark

2.1
, we see that

\|K_{b}\|_{1,\varepsilon}^{2}\leq\|K_{b}\|_{\frac{2}{1+\alpha},\varepsilon}^{2}\varepsilon^{1-\alpha}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \|K_{\sigma}\|_{2,\varepsilon}^{2}\leq\|K_{\sigma}\|_{\frac{2}{\alpha},\varepsilon}^{2}\varepsilon^{1-\alpha}.

(4.17)

Furthermore, noting that
e^{-x}\leq 1\wedge x^{-\beta}
for any
x\geq 0
and
\beta\in(0,1)
, one has, for any
s\in[0,T]
,

\displaystyle\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{1}(0,s)}\leq s\wedge\left(\frac{2\theta^{-(1+\alpha)/2}s^{(1-\alpha)/2}}{1-\alpha}\right)\leq\left(s^{(1+\alpha)/2}\vee\frac{2}{1-\alpha}\right)(1\vee\theta)^{-(1+\alpha)/2}s^{(1-\alpha)/2},

\displaystyle\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{2}(0,s)}\leq\left(s\wedge\left(\frac{(2\theta)^{-\alpha}s^{1-\alpha}}{1-\alpha}\right)\right)^{1/2}\leq\left(s^{\alpha}\vee\frac{1}{2^{\alpha}(1-\alpha)}\right)^{1/2}(1\vee\theta)^{-\alpha/2}s^{(1-\alpha)/2}.

Hence,

\begin{split}&\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)r(\theta)\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{1}(0,s)}^{2}|M_{b}(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\leq C_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)^{-\alpha}r(\theta)|M_{b}(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,s^{1-\alpha},\\
&\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)r(\theta)\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{2}(0,s)}^{2}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\leq C_{\alpha}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)^{1-\alpha}r(\theta)|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,s^{1-\alpha},\\
&\hskip 227.62204pts\in[0,T],\end{split}

(4.18)

where
C_{\alpha}
is a positive constant which depends only on
\alpha
and
T
. Noting the integrability condition (
4.3
) in
Assumption

2
, by (
4.16
), (
4.17
) and (
4.18
) (applying to
s=T
and
s=\varepsilon
), we obtain the desired estimate (
4.13
).
Furthermore, noting that
r(\theta)^{-1}\leq(1+\theta)r(\theta)
, the integrability condition (
4.2
) in
Assumption

2
and the estimate (
4.13
) yield that

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}\big[\|Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\|^{p}_{L^{1}(\mu)}\big]^{1/p}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\|Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}(\theta)\|_{C^{p}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)^{-1}\|Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}(\theta)\|_{C^{p}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})}^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}<\infty.

Similarly, we can show that
\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\mathbb{E}[\|Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\|^{p}_{L^{1}(\mu)}]^{1/p}<\infty
. Hence, the estimate (
4.14
) holds.

Next, we show (
4.15
). Fix
t\in[0,T]
. We integrate left- and right-hand sides of (
4.10
) and (
4.11
) with respect to
\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)
and switch the orders of integrals by means of the (stochastic) Fubini theorem (cf.
[
33
, Theorem 2.2]
). To do so, let us check the integrability conditions which ensures the applications of the stochastic Fubini theorem. Observe that, for any
b\in L^{1,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,t;\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\theta(t-s)}|M_{b}(\theta)||b_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|b\|_{L^{1,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{1}(0,t)}|M_{b}(\theta)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)

\displaystyle\leq\|b\|_{L^{1,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,t)}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)^{-1}\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{1}(0,t)}^{2}|M_{b}(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2},

and the last line is finite thanks to the integrability condition (
4.2
) in
Assumption

2
and (
4.18
), together with
r(\theta)^{-1}\leq(1+\theta)r(\theta)
. Similarly, for any
\sigma\in L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,t;\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\int^{t}_{0}e^{-2\theta(t-s)}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|^{2}|\sigma_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|\sigma\|_{L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,t)}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{2}(0,t)}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)

\displaystyle\leq\|\sigma\|_{L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,t)}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)^{-1}\|e^{-\theta\cdot}\|_{L^{2}(0,t)}^{2}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}<\infty.

Thus, for such processes
b
and
\sigma
, we can apply the (stochastic) Fubini theorem (cf.
[
33
]
), and the expressions (
4.1
) for
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
yield that

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\theta(t-s)}M_{b}(\theta)b_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)=\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}e^{-\theta(t-s)}M_{b}(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,b_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s=\int^{t}_{0}K_{b}(t-s)b_{s}\,\mathrm{d}s\ \ \text{a.s.}

and

\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\theta(t-s)}M_{\sigma}(\theta)\sigma_{s}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)=\int^{t}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}e^{-\theta(t-s)}M_{\sigma}(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\sigma_{s}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}=\int^{t}_{0}K_{\sigma}(t-s)\sigma_{s}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\ \ \text{a.s.}

Applying the above observations to (
4.10
) and noting that
X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
solves the SVE (
3.4
), we get

\displaystyle\mu[Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}]

\displaystyle=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)

\displaystyle=\int^{t}_{0}K_{b}(t-s)\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(s)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}+\delta b^{v}(s)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(s)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int^{t}_{0}K_{\sigma}(t-s)\Big\{\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{s}+\delta\sigma^{v}(s)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(s)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}

\displaystyle=X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}\ \ \text{a.s.}

Similarly, from the expression (
4.11
) for
Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
and the SVE (
3.6
) for
X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
, we obtain
\mu[Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}]=X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{t}
a.s. Thus, (
4.15
) holds. This completes the proof.
∎

4.2
Heuristic derivations of first and second order adjoint equations

In this subsection, we heuristically demonstrate how to derive the adjoint equations. The formal calculations in this subsection will be rigorously proved in
Section

4.4
. As before, suppose that
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

2
hold. Fix
\hat{u},v\in\mathcal{U}
,
\tau\in[0,T)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,T-\tau]
. Let
Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
and
Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
be the lifts of
X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
and
X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
, respectively, defined in the previous subsection. Our idea is to calculate the terms
\mathbb{E}[\hat{h}_{x}X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}]
and
\mathbb{E}[\langle\hat{h}_{xx}X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T},X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}\rangle]
appearing in
J^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
by using semimartingale properties of
Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
and
Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
, respectively.

First of all, noting
Lemma

4.4
, we have

\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{h}_{x}X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}]=\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{h}_{x}\mu[Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}]\big]=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{h}_{x}Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}(\theta)\big]\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)

(4.19)

and

\begin{split}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\hat{h}_{xx}X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T},X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}\big\rangle\Big]&=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\hat{h}_{xx}\mu[Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}],\mu[Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}]\big\rangle\Big]\\
&=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\hat{h}_{xx}Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}(\theta_{2}),Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\Big]\,\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}).\end{split}

(4.20)

Here, we used Fubini’s theorem, which is justified by the integrability conditions (
3.2
) and (
4.14
). We focus on the expectations
\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{h}_{x}Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}(\theta)\big]
and
\mathbb{E}[\langle\hat{h}_{xx}Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}(\theta_{2}),Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}_{T}(\theta_{1})\rangle]
for fixed
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
. Let
\mathbb{R}^{n}
-valued process
(\hat{g}_{t}(\theta))_{t\in[0,T]}
and
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
-valued process
(\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}))_{t\in[0,T]}
be given, and assume that they are progressively measurable and satisfy

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int^{T}_{0}|\hat{g}_{t}(\theta)|\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}+\left(\int^{T}_{0}|\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\right]<\infty.

(4.21)

Consider the following backward stochastic differential equations (BSDEs):

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)=\theta\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t-\hat{g}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t+\hat{q}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
\hat{p}_{T}(\theta)=-\hat{h}_{x}^{\top},\end{cases}

(4.22)

and

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t-\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t+\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
\hat{P}_{T}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=-\hat{h}_{xx}.\end{cases}

(4.23)

Noting that
\hat{h}_{x}^{\top}\in L^{2}_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
\hat{h}_{xx}\in L^{2}_{\mathcal{F}_{T}}(\Omega;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
(see (
3.2
)), by the well-known result on BSDEs (cf.
[
40
, Proposition 4.3.1]
), we see that the above BSDEs admit unique adapted solutions
(\hat{p}(\theta),\hat{q}(\theta))
and
(\hat{P}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),\hat{Q}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}))
, which are
\mathbb{R}^{n}
- and
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
-valued respectively and satisfy

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}|\hat{q}_{t}(\theta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}|\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty.

For a while, as in the proof of
Proposition

3.1
, we sometimes omit the super scripts
v,\tau,\varepsilon
. Noting that
Y^{1,2}(\theta)=Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}(\theta)
is an
\mathbb{R}^{n}
-valued Itô process which satisfies the SDE (
4.9
) (for fixed
\theta
), by using Itô’s formula and recalling the notation (
2.2
), we have

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{h}_{x}Y^{1,2}_{T}(\theta)\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\hat{p}_{T}(\theta),Y^{1,2}_{T}(\theta)\big\rangle-\big\langle\hat{p}_{0}(\theta),Y^{1,2}_{0}(\theta)\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\Big\{\big\langle\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}M_{b}(\theta)^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}M_{\sigma}(\theta)^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}(\theta),X^{1,2}_{t}\big\rangle-\big\langle\hat{g}_{t}(\theta),Y^{1,2}_{t}(\theta)\big\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle\Big(\big\langle M_{b}(\theta)^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}(\theta),\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle M_{\sigma}(\theta)^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}(\theta),\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle\Big)X^{1}_{t},X^{1}_{t}\Big\rangle\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\vphantom{\int^{T}_{0}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\Big\{\big\langle M_{b}(\theta)^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}(\theta),\delta b(t)+\delta b_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle+\big\langle M_{\sigma}(\theta)^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}(\theta),\delta\sigma(t)+\delta\sigma_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right].

(4.24)

Similarly, noting the SDE (
4.8
) for
Y^{1}(\theta_{1})
and
Y^{1}(\theta_{2})
(for fixed
\theta_{1}
and
\theta_{2}
), by using Itô’s formula, we have

\displaystyle-\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\hat{h}_{xx}Y^{1}_{T}(\theta_{2}),Y^{1}_{T}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\hat{P}_{T}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})Y^{1}_{T}(\theta_{2}),Y^{1}_{T}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle-\big\langle\hat{P}_{0}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})Y^{1}_{0}(\theta_{2}),Y^{1}_{0}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\Big\{\big\langle\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}M_{b}(\theta_{1})^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle+\big\langle\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{b}(\theta_{2})\hat{b}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+\big\langle\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+\big\langle\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle+\big\langle\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt-\big\langle\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\vphantom{\int^{T}_{0}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\Big\{\big\langle M_{b}(\theta_{1})^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),\delta b(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{b}(\theta_{2})\delta b(t),Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 85.35826pt+\big\langle M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})\delta\sigma(t),\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 85.35826pt+\big\langle M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 85.35826pt+\big\langle M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})\delta\sigma(t),\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 85.35826pt+\big\langle M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})\delta\sigma(t),Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\vphantom{\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}}\right].

(4.25)

Next, we integrate both sides of (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) with respect to
\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)
and
\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})
, respectively. Heuristically, we assume that the maps
(\omega,t,\theta)\mapsto\hat{g}_{t}(\theta),\hat{p}_{t}(\theta),\hat{q}_{t}(\theta)
and
(\omega,t,\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mapsto\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
are jointly measurable and satisfy suitable integrability conditions that enable us to switch the orders of integrations for
\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{d}t
and
\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\otimes\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\otimes\mathrm{d}\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{d}t
by means of Fubini’s theorem. Recall the notation (
4.12
) of the integral map
\mu[\cdot]
. From (
4.24
), we get (at least formally)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\big[\hat{h}_{x}Y^{1,2}_{T}(\theta)\big]\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\left\{\big\langle\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],X^{1,2}_{t}\big\rangle-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\hat{g}_{t}(\theta),Y^{1,2}_{t}(\theta)\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle\Big(\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle\Big)X^{1}_{t},X^{1}_{t}\Big\rangle\vphantom{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}}\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t\vphantom{\int^{T}_{0}}\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\Big\{\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\delta b(t)+\delta b_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\delta\sigma(t)+\delta\sigma_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right].

(4.26)

Also, from (
4.25
), we get (at least formally)

\displaystyle-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\langle\hat{h}_{xx}Y^{1}_{T}(\theta_{2}),Y^{1}_{T}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\Big]\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\big\langle\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),\delta b(t)\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\delta b(t),Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\delta\sigma(t),\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\delta\sigma(t),\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\delta\sigma(t),Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t\vphantom{\int^{T}_{0}}\right],

(4.27)

where

\displaystyle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M_{b}^{\top}(\theta_{1})\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1}),\ \mu[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{b}(\theta_{2})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}),

\displaystyle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]:=\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}),

and the terms
\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]
and
\mu[\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]
are defined similarly. The above calculations will be proved rigorously after deriving adjoint equations and showing their well-posedness; see
Proposition

4.13
below.

By (
4.19
), (
4.20
), (
4.26
) and (
4.27
), the term
J^{1,2}=J^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
defined by (
3.7
) is expressed as follows:

\displaystyle J^{1,2}

\displaystyle=-\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\left\{\big\langle\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}]-\hat{f}_{x}(t)^{\top},X^{1,2}_{t}\big\rangle-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\hat{g}_{t}(\theta),Y^{1,2}_{t}(\theta)\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt-\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\left\{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\big\langle\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\Big\langle\Big(\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(t)\Big)X^{1}_{t},X^{1}_{t}\Big\rangle\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt-\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt-\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\left\{\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\delta b(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle-\delta f(t)+\frac{1}{2}\big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\delta\sigma(t),\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 8.5359pt-\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\left\{\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\delta b_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\delta\sigma_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),\delta b(t)\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\delta b(t),Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{2}\big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle+\frac{1}{2}\big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\delta\sigma(t),\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\right.\right.

\displaystyle\left.\left.\hskip 42.67912pt+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})+\frac{1}{2}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\delta\sigma(t),Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right].

(4.28)

Notice that the third expectation in the right-hand side of (
4.28
) does not involve
X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
or
X^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
. The last expectation is expected to be of order
o(\varepsilon)
(which will be shown after introducing precise forms of adjoint equations and proving their well-posedness; see
Proposition

4.13
below). Moreover, noting that
\mu[Y^{1}_{t}]=X^{1}_{t}
and
\mu[Y^{1,2}_{t}]=X^{1,2}_{t}
a.s. for any
t\in[0,T]
by
Lemma

4.4
, the first and second expectations would vanish if
\hat{g}
and
\hat{G}
were of the following form:

\hat{g}_{t}(\theta)=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}]-\hat{f}_{x}(t)^{\top}

(4.29)

and

\begin{split}\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})&=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\mu[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(t)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\mu[\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\rangle+\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(t).\end{split}

(4.30)

Inserting the above expressions to the BSDEs (
4.22
) and (
4.23
), we get the
first order adjoint equation

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)=\theta\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t-\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}]-\hat{f}_{x}(t)^{\top}\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t+\hat{q}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\ t\in[0,T],\\
\hat{p}_{T}(\theta)=-\hat{h}_{x}^{\top},\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\end{cases}

(4.31)

and the
second order adjoint equation

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\\
\hskip 56.9055pt-\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\mu[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(t)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\\
\hskip 99.58464pt+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\mu[\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\\
\hskip 99.58464pt+\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(t)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\\
\hskip 56.9055pt+\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ (\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2},\ t\in[0,T],\\
\hat{P}_{T}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=-\hat{h}_{xx},\ \ (\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}.\end{cases}

(4.32)

Notice that the above equations depend only on the fixed control process
\hat{u}\in\mathcal{U}
and is independent of the choices of
v\in\mathcal{U}
,
\tau\in[0,T)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,T-\tau]
.

Summarizing the above heuristic arguments, by using the solutions
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
and
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
of the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
), the term
J^{1,2}=J^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
is written as

J^{1,2}=-\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\left\{\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\delta b(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle-\delta f(t)+\frac{1}{2}\big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\delta\sigma(t),\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]-\mathcal{E},

(4.33)

where
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{v,\tau,\varepsilon}
is the last expectation in the right-hand side of (
4.28
) (defined through the solutions
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
and
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
of the adjoint equations), which is expected to be of order
o(\varepsilon)
. From this, together with
Proposition

3.1
, one can obtain the global maximum principle. In order to rigorously prove the heuristic arguments in this subsection, we need to investigate the following three points:

•

Define appropriate notions of the “solutions” of the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
), and prove their well-posedness.

•

Justify the applications of Fubini’s theorem deriving (
4.26
) and (
4.27
) from (
4.24
) and (
4.25
), respectively.

•

Show that the remainder term
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{v,\tau,\varepsilon}
, the last expectation in the right-hand side of (
4.28
), is of order
o(\varepsilon)
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
.

The first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
) are systems of infinitely many finite dimensional BSDEs for
(\hat{p}(\theta),\hat{q}(\theta))
and
(\hat{P}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}),\hat{Q}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}))
with parameters
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
, respectively, which are coupled via the integral terms with respect to
\mu
. In another perspective, they can be regarded as infinite dimensional
backward stochastic evolution equations
(BSEEs) defined on certain spaces of (univariate and two variate, respectively) functions. In
Section

4.3
, we formulate a general framework of BSEEs and show the well-posedness of the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
) in a unified manner; see also
Section

5
for the proof of the well-posedness of the general BSEE. After that, in
Section

4.4
, we rigorously justify the heuristic arguments developed in this subsection and show the global maximum principle.

4.3
Well-posedness of the adjoint equations

In this subsection, we show the well-posedness of the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
). If the measure
\mu
is finite and if the maps
M_{b}
and
M_{\sigma}
are bounded (that correspond to the case where the kernels
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
are regular), then (
4.31
) and (
4.32
) can be regarded as BSEEs on
L^{2}(\mu;\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
L^{2}(\mu^{\otimes 2};\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
, respectively. Hence, the general results on BSEEs on a (single) Hilbert space (cf.
[
17
]
) can be applied to them. However, this is not the case for the singular kernels in
Assumption

2
. Since the measure
\mu
is typically an infinite measure, the terminal condition
-\hat{h}_{x}^{\top}
or
-\hat{h}_{xx}
does no longer belong to
L^{p}(\mu;\mathbb{R}^{n})
or
L^{p}(\mu^{\otimes 2};\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
for each
p\in[1,\infty)
. Also, there is a difficulty to deal with the well-posedness and continuity (in the operator sense) of the integral maps appearing in the generators of (
4.31
) and (
4.32
). Our idea to overcome these difficulties in the singular kernel case is to formulate the BSEEs on (a family of) weighted
L^{2}
spaces in the spirit of the author’s previous work
[
13
]
, where the infinite dimensional Markovian lifts of SVEs are investigated.

Now let us introduce a suitable framework of BSEEs on weighted
L^{2}
spaces, that contains the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
) as special cases. Let
(\Theta,\Sigma,\nu)
be a countably generated
\sigma
-finite measure space, that is,
\Sigma
is a countably generated
\sigma
-algebra on a (non-empty) set
\Theta
, and
\nu
is a
\sigma
-finite measure on
(\Theta,\Sigma)
. Let
\varpi:\Theta\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a nonnegative
\Sigma
-measurable function, and let
E
be a Euclidean space. For each
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
, we denote by
\mathbb{H}_{\beta}=\mathbb{H}_{\beta}(\Theta,\Sigma,\nu,\varpi;E)
the set of the
\nu
-equivalence classes of
\Sigma
-measurable functions
\psi:\Theta\to E
such that

\|\psi\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\beta}}:=\left(\int_{\Theta}(1+\varpi(\vartheta))^{\beta}|\psi(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\nu(\mathrm{d}\vartheta)\right)^{1/2}<\infty.

(4.34)

For each
\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\in\mathbb{H}_{\beta}
, define

\langle\psi_{1},\psi_{2}\rangle_{\mathbb{H}_{\beta}}:=\int_{\Theta}(1+\varpi(\vartheta))^{\beta}\langle\psi_{1}(\vartheta),\psi_{2}(\vartheta)\rangle\,\nu(\mathrm{d}\vartheta).

Then,
(\mathbb{H}_{\beta},\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\beta}},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{\mathbb{H}_{\beta}})
is a separable Hilbert space
2
2

2

Notice that
\psi\mapsto(1+\varpi)^{\beta/2}\psi
is an isometric isomorphism from
\mathbb{H}_{\beta}
to
L^{2}(\Theta,\Sigma,\nu;E)
. Since
\Sigma
is countably generated and
\nu
is
\sigma
-finite, the Hilbert space
L^{2}(\Theta,\Sigma,\nu;E)
is separable, and hence
\mathbb{H}_{\beta}
is separable as well.
. We equip
\mathbb{H}_{\beta}
with the Borel
\sigma
-algebra
\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{H}_{\beta})
. Clearly, we have the natural continuous embedding
\mathbb{H}_{\beta}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}_{\beta^{\prime}}
for
\beta\geq\beta^{\prime}
. Notice also that
\mathbb{H}_{0}=L^{2}(\Theta,\Sigma,\nu;E)
.

Let an
\mathcal{F}_{T}
-measurable random variable
\Phi:\Omega\to\mathbb{H}_{\beta_{\Phi}}
and a progressively measurable map
\mathcal{G}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{H}_{\beta_{\mathcal{P}}}\times\mathbb{H}_{\beta_{\mathcal{Q}}}\to\mathbb{H}_{\beta_{\mathcal{G}}}
be given, where
\beta_{\Phi},\beta_{\mathcal{G}},\beta_{\mathcal{P}},\beta_{\mathcal{Q}}\in\mathbb{R}
are given parameters. We consider the following BSEE on the weighted
L^{2}
-space:

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{P}_{t}(\vartheta)=\varpi(\vartheta)\mathcal{P}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t-\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}_{t},\mathcal{Q}_{t})(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathcal{Q}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ \vartheta\in\Theta,\ t\in[0,T],\\
\mathcal{P}_{T}(\vartheta)=\Phi(\vartheta),\ \ \vartheta\in\Theta.\end{cases}

(4.35)

The above is a formal expression. The precise notion of the solution is defined as follows.

Definition 4.5
.

Under the above setting, we say that a pair
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
of progressively measurable processes
\mathcal{P}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{\beta_{\mathcal{P}}}
and
\mathcal{Q}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{\beta_{\mathcal{Q}}}
is a
solution
to the BSEE (
4.35
) if there exist (jointly) progressively measurable maps
\bar{p},\bar{q},\bar{g}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to E
such that the following hold:

•

[\bar{p}_{t}(\cdot)]=\mathcal{P}_{t}
,
[\bar{q}_{t}(\cdot)]=\mathcal{Q}_{t}
, and
[\bar{g}_{t}(\cdot)]=\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}_{t},\mathcal{Q}_{t})
a.s. for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
, as well as
[\bar{p}_{T}(\cdot)]=\Phi
a.s., where
[\psi(\cdot)]
denotes the
\nu
-equivalence class of a
\Sigma
-measurable map
\psi:\Theta\to E
;

•

for each fixed
\vartheta\in\Theta
, it holds that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}|\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\left(\int^{T}_{0}|\bar{g}_{t}(\vartheta)|\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\right]<\infty,

(4.36)

and the process
\bar{p}(\vartheta)=(\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta))_{t\in[0,T]}
is an
E
-valued continuous semimartingale satisfying

\mathrm{d}\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)=\varpi(\vartheta)\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t-\bar{g}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t+\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ t\in[0,T],

in the usual Itô’s sense.

We call a tuple
(\bar{p},\bar{q},\bar{g})
satisfying the above properties a
good representative
for the solution
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
.

Remark 4.6
.

Notice that the process
\mathcal{P}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{\beta_{\mathcal{P}}}
itself is not necessarily an
\mathbb{H}_{\beta_{\mathcal{P}}}
-valued semimartingale.

We summarize the above setup and impose the following assumption on the coefficients
\Phi
and
\mathcal{G}
.

Assumption 3
.

Let
(\Theta,\Sigma,\nu)
be a countably generated
\sigma
-finite measure space. Let
\varpi:\Theta\to\mathbb{R}_{+}
be a nonnegative
\Sigma
-measurable function, and let
E
be a Euclidean space. Fix
\alpha\in[0,1)
. Let
\Phi:\Omega\to\mathbb{H}_{0}
be an
\mathcal{F}_{T}
-measurable random variable and
\mathcal{G}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}\to\mathbb{H}_{0}
be a progressively measurable map. Assume that

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{G}(t,0,0)\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty.

(4.37)

Furthermore, assume that there exists a constant
L>0
such that

\|\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})-\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}\leq L\big\{\|\mathcal{P}-\mathcal{P}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}+\|\mathcal{Q}-\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}\big\}

(4.38)

for any
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}),(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})\in\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
and
(\omega,t)\in\Omega\times[0,T]
.

Remark 4.7
.

As we will see below, the parameter
\alpha\in[0,1)
in
Assumption

3
corresponds to the one in
Assumption

2
. We are in particular interested in the “singular case”
\alpha\in(0,1)
.

The following is the main result in this subsection. The proof is postponed to
Section

5
.

Theorem 4.8
.

Let
Assumption

3
hold. Then, the BSEE (
4.35
) has a solution
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
such that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}+\int^{T}_{0}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{1}}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}\|\mathcal{Q}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right.\\
&\left.\hskip 14.22636pt+\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{Q}_{t}\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\\
&\leq C_{T,L,\alpha}\,\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{G}(t,0,0)\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right],\end{split}

(4.39)

where
C_{T,L,\alpha}>0
is a constant depending only on
T
,
L
and
\alpha
. In particular,

\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}_{t},\mathcal{Q}_{t})\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty.

(4.40)

Furthermore, the solution is unique in the following sense: if
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
is another solution of the BSEE (
4.35
) satisfying (
4.40
) with
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
replaced by
(\mathcal{P}^{\prime},\mathcal{Q}^{\prime})
, then
\mathcal{P}^{\prime}_{t}=\mathcal{P}_{t}
in
\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}
and
\mathcal{Q}^{\prime}_{t}=\mathcal{Q}_{t}
in
\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
a.s. for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
.

Remark 4.9
.

Notice the relations

\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}_{0}=L^{2}(\Theta,\Sigma,\nu;E)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}_{-1}.

The terminal random variable
\Phi
and generator
\mathcal{G}
take values in the space
\mathbb{H}_{0}
. If
Assumption

3
holds with
\alpha=0
, then (
4.35
) reduces to a BSEE defined on the Gelfand triplet
\mathbb{H}_{1}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}_{0}\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}_{-1}
, and the well-established result in
[
28
]
is applicable; see also
[
17
,
18
,
23
,
25
,
42
]
for further works on infinite dimensional BSEEs on Gelfand triplets. However, this is not the case when
\alpha\in(0,1)
. The difficulty in the “singular case” is that
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})\mapsto\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
is not necessarily continuous (or even not defined) as a map from
\mathbb{H}_{1}\times\mathbb{H}_{0}
to
\mathbb{H}_{0}
. In this case, the BSEE (
4.35
) is beyond the existing frameworks of BSEEs on Gelfand triplets, and
Theorem

4.8
is a quite non-trivial result.

We apply
Theorem

4.8
to the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
). Let
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

2
hold. Fix
\hat{u}\in\mathcal{U}
. The first order adjoint equation (
4.31
) can be regarded as a BSEE (
4.35
) with

(\Theta,\Sigma,\nu)=(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),r(\theta)\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)),\ \ \varpi(\theta)=\theta,\ \ E=\mathbb{R}^{n},

and

\Phi(\theta)=-\hat{h}_{x}^{\top},\ \ \mathcal{G}(t,p,q)(\theta)=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}p]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}q]-\hat{f}_{x}(t)^{\top}.

(4.41)

For each
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and a Euclidean space
E
, we denote
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\beta}(E):=\mathbb{H}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),r(\theta)\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta),\theta;E)
. We sometimes omit the dependency on
E
when there is no ambiguity. By (
4.2
), the Euclidean space
E
is continuously embedded into
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}(E)=L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),r(\theta)\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta);E)
by the natural injection
p\mapsto(\theta\mapsto p)
. Notice that the condition (
4.3
) means that
M_{b}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}_{-\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
and
M_{\sigma}\in\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
. Observe that, for any
p\in\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}=\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
q\in\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta)||p(\theta)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)^{-1-\alpha}r(\theta)^{-1}|M_{b}(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)^{1+\alpha}r(\theta)|p(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|M_{b}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{-\alpha}}\|p\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}}

(4.42)

and

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)||q(\theta)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)^{-\alpha}r(\theta)^{-1}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)^{\alpha}r(\theta)|q(\theta)|^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|M_{\sigma}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}}\|q\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\alpha}},

(4.43)

where we used the fact that
r(\theta)^{-1}\leq(1+\theta)r(\theta)
. In particular, the map
p\mapsto\mu[M_{b}^{\top}p]
is a bounded linear operator from
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}
to
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}
, and the map
q\mapsto\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}q]
is a bounded linear operator from
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\alpha}
to
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}
. From the above observations, together with (
3.1
) and (
3.2
), we see that the coefficient (
4.41
) satisfies
Assumption

3
(with the same parameter
\alpha\in[0,1)
as in
Assumption

2
). Hence, by
Theorem

4.8
, the first order adjoint equation (
4.31
) has a unique solution
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{g}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty,

(4.44)

where
\hat{g}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}
is given by (
4.29
).

Remark 4.10
.

In the terminology of
[
13
]
,
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}=\mathcal{H}_{\mu}
,
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1}=\mathcal{V}_{\mu}
,
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{-1}=\mathcal{V}^{*}_{\mu}
and
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{2}=\mathcal{D}(A)
.

The second order adjoint equation (
4.31
) can be regarded as a BSEE (
4.35
) with

(\Theta,\Sigma,\nu)=(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}),r(\theta_{1})r(\theta_{2})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})),\ \ \varpi(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\theta_{1}+\theta_{2},\ \ E=\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},

and

\begin{split}&\Phi(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=-\hat{h}_{xx},\\
&\mathcal{G}(t,P,Q)(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}P(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\mu[P(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(t)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}PM_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}Q(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\mu[Q(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\rangle+\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(t).\end{split}

(4.45)

For each
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
and a Euclidean space
E
, we denote
\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\beta}(E):=\mathbb{H}_{\beta}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}),r(\theta_{1})r(\theta_{2})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2}),\theta_{1}+\theta_{2};E)
. We sometimes omit the dependency on
E
when there is no ambiguity. By (
4.2
), the space
\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}(E)=L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+}),r(\theta)\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta);E)
is continuously embedded into
\mathbb{H}^{2}_{0}(E)=L^{2}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2},\mathcal{B}(\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}),r(\theta_{1})r(\theta_{2})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2});E)
by the natural injections

\big(\theta\mapsto p(\theta)\big)\mapsto\big((\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mapsto p(\theta_{1})\big)\ \ \text{and}\ \ \big(\theta\mapsto p(\theta)\big)\mapsto\big((\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mapsto p(\theta_{2})\big).

Namely, we have the continuous embeddings
E\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}(E)\hookrightarrow\mathbb{H}^{2}_{0}(E)
. By similar calculations as above, for any
P\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}=\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
and
Q\in\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}=\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
, we see that

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta_{1})||P(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right)^{2}r(\theta_{2})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|M_{b}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{-\alpha}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta_{1})^{1+\alpha}|P(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|^{2}r(\theta_{1})r(\theta_{2})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|M_{b}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{-\alpha}}\|P\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}}

(4.46)

and

\displaystyle\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||Q(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right)^{2}r(\theta_{2})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|M_{\sigma}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta_{1})^{\alpha}|Q(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|^{2}r(\theta_{1})r(\theta_{2})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\|M_{\sigma}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}}\|Q\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}}.

(4.47)

Similarly, we have

\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|P(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||M_{b}(\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{2}r(\theta_{1})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right)^{1/2}\leq\|M_{b}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{-\alpha}}\|P\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}}

and

\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|Q(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{2}r(\theta_{1})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right)^{1/2}\leq\|M_{\sigma}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}}\|Q\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}}.

Furthermore, noting that
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{2\alpha}}\leq\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}}
for
\alpha\in[0,1)
,

\displaystyle\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||P(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)^{-\alpha}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|^{2}r(\theta)^{-1}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta_{1})^{\alpha}(1+\theta_{2})^{\alpha}|P(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|^{2}r(\theta_{1})r(\theta_{2})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta)^{1-\alpha}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)|^{2}r(\theta)\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}(1+\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})^{2\alpha}|P(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|^{2}r(\theta_{1})r(\theta_{2})\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle=\|M_{\sigma}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}}^{2}\|P\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{2\alpha}}

\displaystyle\leq\|M_{\sigma}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}}^{2}\|P\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}}.

(4.48)

In particular, the maps
P\mapsto((\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mapsto\mu[M_{b}^{\top}P(\cdot,\theta_{2})])
,
P\mapsto((\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mapsto\mu[P(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}])
and
P\mapsto\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}PM_{\sigma}]
are bounded linear operators from
\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}
to
\mathbb{H}^{2}_{0}
, and the maps
Q\mapsto((\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mapsto\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}Q(\cdot,\theta_{2})])
and
Q\mapsto((\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\mapsto\mu[Q(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}])
are bounded linear operators from
\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}
to
\mathbb{H}^{2}_{0}
. From the above observations, together with (
3.1
), (
3.2
) and the estimate (
4.44
) for the solution
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
of the first order adjoint equation (
4.31
), we see that the coefficient (
4.45
) satisfies
Assumption

3
(with the same parameter
\alpha\in[0,1)
as in
Assumption

2
). Hence, by
Theorem

4.8
, the second order adjoint equation (
4.32
) has a unique solution
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{Q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{G}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty,

(4.49)

where
\hat{G}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{2}_{0}
is given by (
4.30
).

Summarizing the above arguments, we obtain the following result.

Proposition 4.11
.

Let
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

2
hold. Fix
\hat{u}\in\mathcal{U}
. Then, there exist unique solutions
(\hat{p},\hat{q}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})\times\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n})
and
(\hat{P},\hat{Q}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})\times\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
to the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
), respectively. Furthermore, the estimates (
4.44
) and (
4.49
) hold.

Remark 4.12
.

Observe that
(\check{P},\check{Q}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})\times\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}(\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
defined by
\check{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}):=\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{2},\theta_{1})^{\top}
and
\check{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}):=\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{2},\theta_{1})^{\top}
satisfies the same BSEE (
4.32
) as
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
. Thus, by the uniqueness of the solution, we see that
(\check{P},\check{Q})=(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
. In particular, the term
\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]
is a symmetric matrix.

4.4
The global maximum principle

Now we return to the analysis on the global maximum principle. Using
Proposition

4.11
on the well-posedness of the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
), we rigorously justify the formal calculations in
Section

4.2
.

Proposition 4.13
.

Suppose that
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

2
hold. Fix
\hat{u}\in\mathcal{U}
. Then, there exists a Lebesgue-null set
\hat{\mathcal{N}}\subset[0,T)
such that, for any
v\in\mathcal{U}
and any
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\hat{\mathcal{N}}
, it holds that

\begin{split}J^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}&=-\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\left\{\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\delta b^{v}(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\delta\sigma^{v}(t)\big\rangle-\delta f^{v}(t)\vphantom{\frac{1}{2}}\right.\right.\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\left.\left.+\frac{1}{2}\big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\delta\sigma^{v}(t),\delta\sigma^{v}(t)\big\rangle\right\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]+o(\varepsilon)\ \ \text{as $\varepsilon\downarrow 0$,}\end{split}

(4.50)

where
J^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
is defined by (
3.7
), and
(\hat{p},\hat{q}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\alpha}
and
(\hat{P},\hat{Q}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}
are the solutions of the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
), respectively.

Proof.

Let
v\in\mathcal{U}
,
\tau\in[0,T)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,T-\tau]
be given. As before, in this proof, we omit the super scripts
v,\tau,\varepsilon
. We denote by
C
a positive constant which is independent of
\tau
and
\varepsilon
(but may depend on
v
). The value of
C
may vary from line to line. We show (
4.50
) by (A) deriving (
4.28
) rigorously and (B) showing that the last expectation in (
4.28
) is of order
o(\varepsilon)
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
for any
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\hat{\mathcal{N}}
, where
\hat{\mathcal{N}}\subset[0,T)
is a Lebesgue-null set independent of the choice of
v
. We identify the solution
(\hat{p},\hat{q}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\alpha}
of the first order adjoint equation (
4.31
) as well as the process
\hat{g}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}
defined by (
4.29
) with a good representative
(\bar{\hat{p}},\bar{\hat{q}},\bar{\hat{g}}):\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
(see
Definition

4.5
). Also, we identify the solution
(\hat{P},\hat{Q}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}
of the second order adjoint equation (
4.32
) as well as the process
\hat{G}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}^{2}_{0}
defined by (
4.30
) with a good representative
(\bar{\hat{P}},\bar{\hat{Q}},\bar{\hat{G}}):\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
. These identifications do not matter in this proof since the objective (
4.28
) is independent of the choice of the good representatives. By the same reason, we may assume that
\hat{p}_{T}(\theta)=-\hat{h}_{x}^{\top}
a.s. for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and
\hat{P}_{T}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=-\hat{h}_{xx}
a.s. for any
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
.

(A) Rigorous derivation of (
4.28
).
By
Definition

4.5
, we see that the generators
\hat{g}(\theta)
and
\hat{G}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
defined by (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) (through the solutions
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
and
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
) satisfy the integrability condition (
4.21
). Thus, by the arguments in
Section

4.2
, the equalities (
4.24
) and (
4.25
) hold for any
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
and any
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
, respectively. In order to show (
4.28
) rigorously, it remains to justify the applications of Fubini’s theorem deriving (
4.26
) and (
4.27
) from (
4.24
) and (
4.25
), respectively.

(A-1) Justification of Fubini’s theorem deriving (
4.26
) from (
4.24
).
In order to justify the derivation of (
4.26
) from (
4.24
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\hat{g}_{t}(\theta)||Y^{1,2}_{t}(\theta)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty,

(4.51)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta)||\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)||\xi_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty\ \ \text{for any $\xi\in L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})$, and}

(4.52)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)||\hat{q}_{t}(\theta)||\xi_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty\ \ \text{for any $\xi\in L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})$.}

(4.53)

First, we show (
4.51
). Noting
r(\theta)^{-1}\leq(1+\theta)r(\theta)
,
\alpha\in[0,1)
and the estimates (
4.44
) for
\hat{g}
and (
4.13
) for
Y^{1,2}=Y^{1,2,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality and Tonelli’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\hat{g}_{t}(\theta)||Y^{1,2}_{t}(\theta)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)^{-1}\|Y^{1,2}(\theta)\|_{C^{2}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)\left(\int^{T}_{0}\mathbb{E}\big[|\hat{g}_{t}(\theta)|^{2}\big]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{(1-\alpha)/2}\left(\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{g}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

Hence, (
4.51
) holds. Next, we show (
4.52
) and (
4.53
). Let
\xi\in L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Noting (
4.42
) and (
4.44
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta)||\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)||\xi_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi\|_{L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\int^{T}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta)||\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi\|_{L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\left(\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\|M_{b}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{-\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

Hence, (
4.52
) holds. Similarly, noting (
4.43
) and (
4.44
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)||\hat{q}_{t}(\theta)||\xi_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi\|_{L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\int^{T}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)||\hat{q}_{t}(\theta)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi\|_{L^{2,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\left(\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\|M_{\sigma}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

Hence, (
4.53
) holds. The above observations justify the derivation of (
4.26
) from (
4.24
) by means of Fubini’s theorem, and the proof of (A-1) is completed.

(A-2) Justification of Fubini’s theorem deriving (
4.27
) from (
4.25
).
In order to justify the derivation of (
4.27
) from (
4.25
), noting the symmetry of
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
(see
Remark

4.12
), it suffices to show that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty,

(4.54)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta_{1})||\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2})||\xi_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty\ \ \text{for any $\xi\in L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})$,}

(4.55)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2})||\xi_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty\ \ \text{for any $\xi\in L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})$, and}

(4.56)

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})||\xi^{1}_{t}||\xi^{2}_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty\ \ \text{for any $\xi^{1},\xi^{2}\in L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})$.}

(4.57)

First, we show (
4.54
). Noting the estimates (
4.49
) for
\hat{G}
and (
4.13
) for
Y^{1}=Y^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}
, by Hölder’s inequality and Tonelli’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{1})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)^{-1}\|Y^{1}(\theta)\|_{C^{4}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta_{1})r(\theta_{2})\left(\int^{T}_{0}\mathbb{E}\big[|\hat{G}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|^{2}\big]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{1-\alpha}\left(\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{G}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

Hence, (
4.54
) holds. Next, we show (
4.55
) and (
4.56
). Let
\xi\in L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Noting (
4.13
), (
4.46
) and (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta_{1})||\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2})||\xi_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi\|_{L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)^{-1}\|Y^{1}(\theta)\|_{C^{4}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta_{2})\left(\int^{T}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta_{1})||\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{(1-\alpha)/2}\|\xi\|_{L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\left(\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\|M_{b}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{-\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

Hence, (
4.55
) holds. Also, noting (
4.13
), (
4.47
) and (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2})||\xi_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi\|_{L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta)^{-1}\|Y^{1}(\theta)\|_{C^{4}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\times\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}r(\theta_{2})\left(\int^{T}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{1/2}

\displaystyle\leq C\varepsilon^{(1-\alpha)/2}\|\xi\|_{L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\left(\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\|M_{\sigma}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{Q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

Hence, (
4.56
) holds. Lastly, we show (
4.57
). Let
\xi^{1},\xi^{2}\in L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n})
. Noting (
4.13
), (
4.48
) and (
4.49
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})||\xi^{1}_{t}||\xi^{2}_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi^{1}\|_{L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\|\xi^{2}\|_{L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\int^{T}_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\right)^{2}\right]^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}t

\displaystyle\leq\|\xi^{1}\|_{L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\|\xi^{2}\|_{L^{4,\infty}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\left(\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\|M_{\sigma}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1-\alpha}}^{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{2}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}

\displaystyle<\infty.

Hence, (
4.57
) holds. The above observations justify the derivation of (
4.27
) from (
4.25
) by means of Fubini’s theorem, and the proof of (A-2) is completed.

The observations in (A) above rigorously prove the equality (
4.28
). By the relations (
4.29
) and (
4.30
) for
\hat{g}(\theta)
and
\hat{G}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
, we see that the first and second expectations in the right-hand side of (
4.28
) are zeros. Consequently, noting the symmetry of
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
(see
Remark

4.12
), we obtain the expression (
4.33
), where
\mathcal{E}=\mathcal{E}^{v,\tau,\varepsilon}\in\mathbb{R}
is given by

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}

\displaystyle:=\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\Big\{\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\delta b_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\rangle+\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\delta\sigma_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\big\rangle\vphantom{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),\delta b(t)\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})+\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2}),\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\vphantom{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 56.9055pt+\big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t},\delta\sigma(t)\big\rangle\vphantom{\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}}\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right],

which is nothing but the last expectation in the right-hand side of (
4.28
).

(B) Convergence with order
o(\varepsilon)
of the last expectation in the right-hand side of (
4.28
).
In order to show (
4.50
), it remains to prove that there exists a Lebesgue-null set
\hat{\mathcal{N}}\subset[0,T)
, which is independent of the choice of
v\in\mathcal{U}
, such that
\mathcal{E}^{v,\tau,\varepsilon}=o(\varepsilon)
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
for any
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\hat{\mathcal{N}}
. For this purpose, it suffices to show that the following hold:

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta)||\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)||\delta b_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]=0,

(4.58)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta)||\hat{q}_{t}(\theta)||\delta\sigma_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]=0,

(4.59)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta_{1})||\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2})||\delta b(t)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]=0,

(4.60)

\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||Y^{1}_{t}(\theta_{2})||\delta\sigma(t)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]=0,\ \ \text{and}

(4.61)
