\displaystyle\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{\sigma}(\theta_{1})||\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})||M_{\sigma}(\theta_{2})||\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}||\delta\sigma(t)|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{1})\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t\right]=0,

(4.62)

for any
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\hat{\mathcal{N}}
for some Lebesgue-null set
\hat{\mathcal{N}}\subset[0,T)
which is independent of the choice of
v
. The above five claims are shown by applying the arguments we have proved so far. Let us prove (
4.58
). Applying the argument showing (
4.52
) above to
\xi_{t}=\delta b_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[\tau,\tau+\varepsilon]}(t)
and noting
|\delta b_{x}(t)|\leq 2\kappa
by
Assumption

1
, we see that, for any
v\in\mathcal{U}
,
\tau\in[0,T)
and
\varepsilon\in(0,T-\tau]
,

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\int_{\mathbb{R}_{+}}|M_{b}(\theta)||\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)||\delta b_{x}(t)X^{1}_{t}|\,\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq 2\kappa\|X^{1}\|_{C^{2}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}\left(\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}(T-t)^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{1/2}\|M_{b}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{-\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}.

By (
3.9
) in
Proposition

3.1
, we have
\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\|X^{1,v,\tau,\varepsilon}\|_{C^{2}_{\mathbb{F}}(0,T)}=0
for any
v\in\mathcal{U}
and any
\tau\in[0,T)
. Also, we have
\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}(T-t)^{-\alpha}\,\mathrm{d}t=(T-\tau)^{-\alpha}<\infty
for any
\tau\in[0,T)
. Lastly, noting the estimate (
4.44
) for
\hat{p}
, by Lebesgue’s differentiation theorem, there exists a Lebesgue-null set
\hat{\mathcal{N}}\subset[0,T)
, which does not depend on
v
, such that

\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\hat{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]=(T-\tau)^{\alpha}\mathbb{E}\Big[\|\hat{p}_{\tau}\|_{\mathbb{H}^{1}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\Big]<\infty

for any
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\hat{\mathcal{N}}
. Hence, we see that (
4.58
) holds for any
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\hat{\mathcal{N}}
. Similarly, we can show (
4.59
), (
4.60
), (
4.61
) and (
4.62
) by using the arguments showing (
4.53
), (
4.55
), (
4.56
) and (
4.57
) above, respectively.

Consequently, we have
\mathcal{E}^{v,\tau,\varepsilon}=o(\varepsilon)
as
\varepsilon\downarrow 0
for any
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\hat{\mathcal{N}}
for some Lebesgue-null set
\hat{\mathcal{N}}\subset[0,T)
, which is independent of the choice of
v
. This completes the proof of
Proposition

4.13
.
∎

Remark 4.14
.

Unlike the last assertion of
Proposition

3.1
,
Proposition

4.13
holds true without assuming that
K_{b}\in L^{3/2}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
or
K_{\sigma}\in L^{6}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
.

Recall the definition (
1.6
) of the
Hamiltonian

H:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\to\mathbb{R}
:

H(t,u,x,p,q):=\langle p,b(t,u,x)\rangle+\langle q,\sigma(t,u,x)\rangle-f(t,u,x),

for
(\omega,t,u,x,p,q)\in\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}\times\mathbb{R}^{n}
, where we suppressed the dependency on
\omega
. This is the “standard” Hamiltonian appearing in control problems of SDEs; see
[
27
]
and
[
39
, Chapter 3]
. Using the Hamiltonian, the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
) can be written as (
1.10
) and (
1.11
), respectively.

Combining
Proposition

3.1
and
Proposition

4.13
, we obtain the following
global maximum principle
, which is the main result of this paper.

Theorem 4.15
.

Suppose that
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

2
with
\alpha=\frac{1}{3}
hold. Let
\hat{u}\in\mathcal{U}
be given, and denote
\hat{X}:=X^{\hat{u}}
. Also, let
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
and
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
be the solutions of the associated first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
), respectively. If
\hat{u}
is an optimal control of the stochastic Volterra control problem (
1.1
)–(
1.2
), then the following variational inequality holds:

\begin{split}&H\big(t,\hat{u}_{t},\hat{X}_{t},\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}]\big)-H\big(t,v,\hat{X}_{t},\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}]\big)\\
&-\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\big\{\sigma(t,\hat{u}_{t},\hat{X}_{t})-\sigma(t,v,\hat{X}_{t})\big\},\sigma(t,\hat{u}_{t},\hat{X}_{t})-\sigma(t,v,\hat{X}_{t})\Big\rangle\geq 0\\
&\hskip 113.81102pt\text{for all $v\in U$ a.s.\ for a.e.\ $t\in[0,T]$}.\end{split}

(4.63)

Proof.

By
Lemma

4.1
,
Assumption

2
with
\alpha=\frac{1}{3}
implies that
K_{b}\in L^{3/2}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
and
K_{\sigma}\in L^{6}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
. Hence, the last assertion in
Proposition

3.1
holds.

Assume that
\hat{u}\in\mathcal{U}
is an optimal control. Define the so-called
\mathcal{H}
-function
\hat{\mathcal{H}}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times U\to\mathbb{R}
by

\displaystyle\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(v)

\displaystyle:=H\big(t,v,\hat{X}_{t},\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}]\big)

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\frac{1}{2}\Big\langle\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\big\{\sigma(t,\hat{u}_{t},\hat{X}_{t})-\sigma(t,v,\hat{X}_{t})\big\},\sigma(t,\hat{u}_{t},\hat{X}_{t})-\sigma(t,v,\hat{X}_{t})\Big\rangle,

for
(\omega,t,v)\in\Omega\times[0,T]\times U
, where we suppressed the dependency on
\omega
. It can be easily shown that the
\mathcal{H}
-function is progressively measurable and satisfies
\mathbb{E}[\int^{T}_{0}|\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(v_{t})|\,\mathrm{d}t]<\infty
for any
v\in\mathcal{U}
. Furthermore, by the continuity conditions (with respect to
u\in U
) in
Assumption

1
, we see that
v\mapsto\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(v)
is continuous for any
(\omega,t)\in\Omega\times[0,T]
. Notice that the variational inequality (
4.63
) is equivalent to the following maximum condition:

\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(\hat{u}_{t})=\max_{v\in U}\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(v)\ \ \text{a.s.\ for a.e.\ $t\in[0,T]$}.

(4.64)

In the following, we will show (
4.64
).

Noting that the control domain
U
is separable (by
Assumption

1
), we can take a countable dense subset
\{v^{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
of
U
. Each
v^{\ell}\in U
can be seen as a constant control process
v^{\ell}_{t}:=v^{\ell}
,
t\in[0,T]
, which belongs to
\mathcal{U}
thanks to
Assumption

1
. Notice that (the
\mathbb{P}
-equivalence classes of) the functions
t\mapsto\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(\hat{u}_{t})
and
t\mapsto\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(v^{\ell})
are Bochner-integrable as maps from
[0,T]
to the separable Banach space
L^{1}(\Omega,\mathcal{F}_{T},\mathbb{P})
. Hence, by Lebesgue’s differentiation theorem for Bochner-integrable functions (cf.
[
16
, Theorem 3.8.5]
), there exists a Lebesgue-null set
\check{\mathcal{N}}\subset[0,T)
such that

\lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\big[|\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(\hat{u}_{t})-\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(\hat{u}_{\tau})|\big]\,\mathrm{d}t=0\ \ \text{and}\ \ \lim_{\varepsilon\downarrow 0}\frac{1}{\varepsilon}\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\mathbb{E}\big[|\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(v^{\ell})-\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(v^{\ell})|\big]\,\mathrm{d}t=0

(4.65)

for any
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
and any
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\check{\mathcal{N}}
. Let
\mathcal{N}:=\hat{\mathcal{N}}\cup\check{\mathcal{N}}
, where
\hat{\mathcal{N}}\subset[0,T)
is the Lebesgue-null set specified in
Proposition

4.13
. Here, we emphasize that the null set
\mathcal{N}
depends only on the fixed (optimal) control
\hat{u}
and the sequence
\{v^{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
.

Let
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
,
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\mathcal{N}
and
A_{\tau}\in\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
be given. Define

v^{\ell,\tau}_{t}(\omega):=v^{\ell}\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{A_{\tau}\times[\tau,T]}(\omega,t)+\hat{u}_{t}(\omega)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{(\Omega\times[0,T])\setminus(A_{\tau}\times[\tau,T])}(\omega,t).

Notice that
v^{\ell,\tau}\in\mathcal{U}
. Since
K_{b}\in L^{3/2}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
and
K_{\sigma}\in L^{6}(0,T;\mathbb{R}^{n\times n})
, by the last assertion in
Proposition

3.1
, we have

J(u^{v^{\ell,\tau},\tau,\varepsilon})-J(\hat{u})=J^{1,2,v^{\ell,\tau},\tau,\varepsilon}+o(\varepsilon)\ \ \text{as $\varepsilon\downarrow 0$},

where
u^{v^{\ell,\tau},\tau,\varepsilon}\in\mathcal{U}
and
J^{1,2,v^{\ell,\tau},\tau,\varepsilon}
are defined by (
3.3
) and (
3.7
), respectively, with the choice
v=v^{\ell,\tau}
. Furthermore, noting that
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\mathcal{N}
, by
Proposition

4.13
and the definition of the
\mathcal{H}
-function, we see that

J^{1,2,v^{\ell,\tau},\tau,\varepsilon}=\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\Big\{\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(\hat{u}_{t})-\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(v^{\ell,\tau}_{t})\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]+o(\varepsilon)\ \ \text{as $\varepsilon\downarrow 0$}.

However, by the definition of
v^{\ell,\tau}
and (
4.65
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\Big\{\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(\hat{u}_{t})-\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(v^{\ell,\tau}_{t})\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{\tau+\varepsilon}_{\tau}\Big\{\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(\hat{u}_{t})-\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{t}(v^{\ell})\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{A_{\tau}}\right]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(\hat{u}_{\tau})-\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(v^{\ell})\big\}\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{A_{\tau}}\Big]\,\varepsilon+o(\varepsilon)\ \ \text{as $\varepsilon\downarrow 0$}.

Consequently, we have

J(u^{v^{\ell,\tau},\tau,\varepsilon})-J(\hat{u})=\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(\hat{u}_{\tau})-\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(v^{\ell})\big\}\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{A_{\tau}}\Big]\,\varepsilon+o(\varepsilon)\ \ \text{as $\varepsilon\downarrow 0$}.

By the optimality of
\hat{u}
, we have
J(u^{v^{\ell,\tau},\tau,\varepsilon})-J(\hat{u})\geq 0
for any
\varepsilon\in(0,T-\tau]
, and hence it holds that

\mathbb{E}\Big[\big\{\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(\hat{u}_{\tau})-\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(v^{\ell})\big\}\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{A_{\tau}}\Big]\geq 0.

Since
A_{\tau}\in\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
is arbitrary, noting that
\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(\hat{u}_{\tau})
and
\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(v^{\ell})
are
\mathcal{F}_{\tau}
-measurable, we have
\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(\hat{u}_{\tau})\geq\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(v^{\ell})
a.s. Furthermore, since
\ell\in\mathbb{N}
is arbitrary, the continuity of
v\mapsto\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(v)
and the density of
\{v^{\ell}\}_{\ell\in\mathbb{N}}
in
U
yield that

\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(\hat{u}_{\tau})=\max_{v\in U}\hat{\mathcal{H}}_{\tau}(v)\ \ \text{a.s.}

The above holds for any
\tau\in[0,T)\setminus\mathcal{N}
with
\mathcal{N}\subset[0,T)
being null with respect to the Lebesgue measure. Thus, the maximum condition (
4.64
) holds. This completes the proof.
∎

Remark 4.16
.

•

The above result is a natural generalization of Peng’s global maximum principle for SDEs
[
27
]
(see also
[
39
, Chapter 3]
). Indeed, if
K_{b}(t)=K_{\sigma}(t)=I_{n\times n}
, then we can take
\mu(\mathrm{d}\theta)=\delta_{0}(\mathrm{d}\theta)
and
M_{b}(\theta)=M_{\sigma}(\theta)=I_{n\times n}
. In this case, the first and second order adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
) (with
\theta=0
and
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=(0,0)
, respectively) as well as the variational inequality (
4.63
) reduce to the counterparts in the SDEs case.

•

Wang and Yong
[
35
]
obtained a different form of a global maximum principle for non-convolution type SVEs with regular kernels. Their approach relies on the analysis of backward stochastic Volterra integral equations (BSVIEs); the first order adjoint equation is written as a Type-II BSVIE (see (
B.1
) in
Appendix

B
), and the second order adjoint equation is written as a coupled system of non-standard BSVIEs (see (
B.4
) in
Appendix

B
). Compared with
[
35
]
, our approach does not need the analysis of BSVIEs. The adjoint equations we obtained in the present paper is much simpler than that of
[
35
]
, and is more tractable (especially in the singular kernel case) thanks to the semimartingale structure. As a price, unlike
[
35
]
, we require the structure condition (
Assumption

2
) on the kernels. In
Appendix

B
, we show some relationships between BSEEs and BSVIEs.

5
Well-posedness of general BSEEs on weighted
L^{2}
spaces

In this section, we prove
Theorem

4.8
. To do so, the following novel estimate for finite dimensional BSDEs, which is interesting by its own right, plays a key role.

Lemma 5.1
.

There exists a universal constant
C_{0}>0
such that the following holds. Let
E
be a Euclidean space. Let
\mathfrak{h}:\Omega\to E
be an
\mathcal{F}_{T}
-measurable random variable and
\mathfrak{g}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to E
be a progressively measurable process such that

\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{g}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty,

(5.1)

where
\alpha\in[0,1)
is a given constant. Fix a constant
\kappa>0
, and let
(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
be the adapted solution of the following BSDE on
E
:

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\mathfrak{p}_{t}=\kappa\mathfrak{p}_{t}\mathrm{d}t-\mathfrak{g}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathfrak{q}_{t}\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
\mathfrak{p}_{T}=\mathfrak{h}.\end{cases}

(5.2)

Then,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+\kappa\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{q}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right.\\
&\left.\hskip 28.45274pt+\kappa^{\alpha}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+\kappa^{1+\alpha}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\kappa^{\alpha}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{q}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\\
&\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\kappa^{1-\alpha}}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{g}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right],\end{split}

(5.3)

where
\Gamma(\beta):=\int^{\infty}_{0}t^{\beta-1}e^{-t}\,\mathrm{d}t
,
\beta>0
, denotes the Gamma function.

Remark 5.2
.

Under the condition (
5.1
) with
\alpha\in[0,1)
, by the Cauchy–Schwarz inequality,

\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{g}_{t}|\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\right]\leq\frac{T^{1-\alpha}}{1-\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{g}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty.

Hence, by
[
40
, Proposition 4.3.1]
, there exists a unique adapted solution
(\mathfrak{p},\mathfrak{q})
to the BSDE (
5.2
) such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{q}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty.

The important point in the statement of
Lemma

5.1
is that the constant
C_{0}
in (
5.3
) is independent of the parameter
\kappa
.

Proof of
Lemma

5.1
.

In this proof, we denote by
C
a universal positive constant which varies from line to line.

First, we show that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+\kappa\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{q}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\leq C\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}||\mathfrak{g}_{t}|\,\mathrm{d}t\right].

(5.4)

By Itô’s formula, we have

|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+2\kappa\int^{T}_{t}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{t}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s=|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+2\int^{T}_{t}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{g}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s-2\int^{T}_{t}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{q}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},\ \ t\in[0,T].

(5.5)

On the one hand, taking the expectations in both sides of (
5.5
) with
t=0
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[2\kappa\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+2\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right].

(5.6)

On the other hand, taking the supremum in
t\in[0,T]
and then taking the expectations in both sides of (
5.5
), the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality and Young’s inequality yield that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+2\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]+C\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+2\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]+C\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\right].

Hence, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\right]\leq C\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]+C\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right].

(5.7)

By (
5.6
) and (
5.7
), we obtain (
5.4
). In particular, in the case of
\alpha=0
, Young’s inequality yields that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+\kappa\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{q}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\leq C\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+\frac{1}{\kappa}\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{g}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]+\frac{\kappa}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right],

and hence the desired estimate (
5.3
) holds in this special case. In the rest of this proof, we assume that
\alpha\in(0,1)
.

Next, we show that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+\kappa\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{q}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\\
&\leq C\mathbb{E}\left[\alpha\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha-1}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}||\mathfrak{g}_{t}|\,\mathrm{d}t\right].\end{split}

(5.8)

From (
5.5
), we have

|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+2\kappa\int^{\tau}_{t}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{\tau}_{t}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s=|\mathfrak{p}_{\tau}|^{2}+2\int^{\tau}_{t}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{g}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s-2\int^{\tau}_{t}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{q}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},\ \ 0\leq t\leq\tau\leq T.

Noting that
\alpha\in(0,1)
, multiplying
\alpha(T-\tau)^{\alpha-1}
to both sides above and then integrating with respect to
\tau\in[t,T]
, we have

\begin{split}&(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+2\kappa\int^{T}_{t}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{t}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&=\alpha\int^{T}_{t}(T-s)^{\alpha-1}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+2\int^{T}_{t}(T-s)^{\alpha}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{g}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s-2\int^{T}_{t}(T-s)^{\alpha}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{q}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},\ \ t\in[0,T].\end{split}

(5.9)

Here, we used
\int^{T}_{t}\alpha(T-\tau)^{\alpha-1}\,\mathrm{d}\tau=(T-t)^{\alpha}
and the (stochastic) Fubini theorem. The applications of the (stochastic) Fubini theorem are justified thanks to the facts that
\mathbb{E}[\sup_{s\in[t,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}]<\infty
,
\mathbb{E}[\int^{T}_{t}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s]<\infty
and
\mathbb{E}[(\int^{T}_{t}|\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s)^{2}]<\infty
; for example, observe that

\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{t}\alpha(T-\tau)^{\alpha-1}\left(\int^{\tau}_{t}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\right]\leq(T-t)^{\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[t,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\right]^{1/2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{t}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]^{1/2}<\infty,

and hence
[
33
, Theorem 2.2]
ensures the interchangeability of the stochastic integral and the Lebesgue integral;

\int^{T}_{t}\alpha(T-\tau)^{\alpha-1}\int^{\tau}_{t}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{q}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\,\mathrm{d}\tau=\int^{T}_{t}\int^{T}_{s}\alpha(T-\tau)^{\alpha-1}\,\mathrm{d}\tau\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{q}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}=\int^{T}_{t}(T-s)^{\alpha}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{q}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\ \ \text{a.s.}

Other terms can be justified similarly. On the one hand, taking the expectations in both sides of (
5.9
) with
t=0
, we have

\mathbb{E}\left[2\kappa\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\alpha\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha-1}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+2\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right].

(5.10)

On the other hand, taking the supremum in
t\in[0,T]
and then taking the expectations in both sides of (
5.9
), the Burkholder–Davis–Gundy inequality and Young’s inequality yield that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\alpha\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha-1}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+2\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]+C\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{2\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right)^{1/2}\right]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\alpha\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha-1}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+2\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]+C\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{1}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{s\in[0,T]}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\right].

Hence,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\right]\\
&\leq C\mathbb{E}\left[\alpha\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha-1}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]+C\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s\right].\end{split}

(5.11)

By (
5.10
) and (
5.11
), we obtain (
5.8
).

Next, we show that

\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\beta-1}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\leq(2\kappa)^{-\beta}\Gamma(\beta)\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+2\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}||\mathfrak{g}_{t}|\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\ \ \text{for any $\beta\in(0,1)$.}

(5.12)

Let
\beta\in(0,1)
be fixed. From (
5.5
), by using Itô’s formula, we have

|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-2\kappa(s-t)}|\mathfrak{q}_{s}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s=e^{-2\kappa(T-t)}|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+2\int^{T}_{t}e^{-2\kappa(s-t)}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{g}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}s-2\int^{T}_{t}e^{-2\kappa(s-t)}\langle\mathfrak{p}_{s},\mathfrak{q}_{s}\rangle\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},

and hence

\mathbb{E}\big[|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\big]\leq\mathbb{E}\left[e^{-2\kappa(T-t)}|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+2\int^{T}_{t}e^{-2\kappa(s-t)}|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right]

for any
t\in[0,T]
. Multiplying
(T-t)^{\beta-1}
to both sides in the above inequality and then integrating with respect to
t\in[0,T]
, we have, by Tonelli’s theorem,

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\beta-1}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\\
&\leq\mathbb{E}\left[\left(\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\beta-1}e^{-2\kappa(T-t)}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)\,|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+2\int^{T}_{0}\left(\int^{s}_{0}(T-t)^{\beta-1}e^{-2\kappa(s-t)}\,\mathrm{d}t\right)\,|\mathfrak{p}_{s}||\mathfrak{g}_{s}|\,\mathrm{d}s\right].\end{split}

(5.13)

Noting that
\beta\in(0,1)
, we have

\int^{s}_{0}(T-t)^{\beta-1}e^{-2\kappa(s-t)}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\int^{s}_{0}(s-t)^{\beta-1}e^{-2\kappa(s-t)}\,\mathrm{d}t\leq\int^{\infty}_{0}t^{\beta-1}e^{-2\kappa t}\,\mathrm{d}t=(2\kappa)^{-\beta}\Gamma(\beta)

for any
s\in[0,T]
. From this estimate and (
5.13
), we obtain (
5.12
).

By (
5.4
), (
5.8
) and (
5.12
) (applying to
\beta=1-\alpha
and
\beta=\alpha
), noting that
\alpha\Gamma(\alpha)\leq 1\leq\Gamma(1-\alpha)
and using Young’s inequality, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+\kappa\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{q}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]+\frac{\kappa^{1-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{-\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\kappa^{\alpha}\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}+\kappa\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{q}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}||\mathfrak{g}_{t}|\,\mathrm{d}t+\kappa^{\alpha}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}||\mathfrak{g}_{t}|\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbb{E}\left[|\mathfrak{h}|^{2}+\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\kappa^{1-\alpha}}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{g}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\frac{\kappa^{1-\alpha}}{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{-\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]+\frac{\kappa^{1+\alpha}}{2}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}|\mathfrak{p}_{t}|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right].

Hence, the desired estimate (
5.3
) holds. This completes the proof.
∎

Next, we show the well-posedness and provides a moment estimate for the BSEE (
4.35
) assuming that the generator
\mathcal{G}
is independent of the solution. Recall the definition (
4.34
) of the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\beta}}
of the Hilbert space
\mathbb{H}_{\beta}
for
\beta\in\mathbb{R}
.

Lemma 5.3
.

Let
Assumption

3
hold, and assume furthermore that
\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})=\mathcal{G}(t,0,0)=:\mathcal{G}_{t}
for any
(\omega,t,\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})\in\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
. Then, there exists a unique solution
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}):\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
to the corresponding BSEE:

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{P}_{t}(\vartheta)=\varpi(\vartheta)\mathcal{P}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t-\mathcal{G}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathcal{Q}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ \vartheta\in\Theta,\ t\in[0,T],\\
\mathcal{P}_{T}(\vartheta)=\Phi(\vartheta),\ \ \vartheta\in\Theta.\end{cases}

(5.14)

Furthermore, for any
\lambda\geq 1
, it holds that

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{Q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right.\\
&\left.\hskip 28.45274pt+\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{Q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\\
&\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{2\lambda T}\|\Phi\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}+\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{G}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right],\end{split}

(5.15)

where
C_{0}>0
is the constant appearing in
Lemma

5.1
.

Proof.

Uniqueness of the solution of the BSEE (
5.14
).
Let
(\mathcal{P}^{1},\mathcal{Q}^{1})
and
(\mathcal{P}^{2},\mathcal{Q}^{2})
be two solutions of the BSEE (
5.14
) with respective good representatives
(\bar{p}^{1},\bar{q}^{1},\bar{g}^{1})
and
(\bar{p}^{2},\bar{q}^{2},\bar{g}^{2})
; see
Definition

4.5
. By the definition, there exists
\Theta_{*}\in\Sigma
with
\nu(\Theta\setminus\Theta_{*})=0
such that, for any
\vartheta\in\Theta_{*}
,
\bar{p}^{1}_{T}(\vartheta)=\bar{p}^{2}_{T}(\vartheta)
a.s. and
\bar{g}^{1}_{t}(\vartheta)=\bar{g}^{2}_{t}(\vartheta)
a.s. for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
. Noting the integrability condition (
4.36
) in
Definition

4.5
, for any
\vartheta\in\Theta_{*}
, the pairs
(\bar{p}^{1}(\vartheta),\bar{q}^{1}(\vartheta))
and
(\bar{p}^{2}(\vartheta),\bar{q}^{2}(\vartheta))
are adapted solutions of the same BSDE on the Euclidean space
E
. By the uniqueness of the adapted solution (cf.
[
40
, Theorem 4.3.1]
), we see that
\bar{p}^{1}_{t}(\vartheta)=\bar{p}^{2}_{t}(\vartheta)
and
\bar{q}^{1}_{t}(\vartheta)=\bar{q}^{2}_{t}(\vartheta)
a.s. for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
for any
\vartheta\in\Theta_{*}
. Since
[\bar{p}^{i}_{t}(\cdot)]=\mathcal{P}^{i}_{t}
and
[\bar{q}^{i}_{t}(\cdot)]=\mathcal{Q}^{i}_{t}
a.s. for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
(where
[\psi(\cdot)]
denotes the
\nu
-equivalence class of a measurable function
\psi:\Theta\to E
), we see that
(\mathcal{P}^{1}_{t},\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{t})=(\mathcal{P}^{2}_{t},\mathcal{Q}^{2}_{t})
in
\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}\times\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
a.s. for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
. This shows the uniqueness of the solution to the BSEE (
5.14
).

Existence of the solution of the BSEE (
5.14
) satisfying (
5.15
).
By
Lemma

A.1
(ii), there exist
\mathcal{F}_{T}\otimes\Sigma
-measurable map
\bar{\varphi}:\Omega\times\Theta\to E
and (jointly) progressively measurable map
\bar{g}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to E
such that
[\bar{\varphi}(\cdot)]=\Phi
and
[\bar{g}_{t}(\cdot)]=\mathcal{G}_{t}
for any
t\in[0,T]
and
\omega\in\Omega
. By (
4.37
) (with
\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})=\mathcal{G}_{t}
) and
Lemma

A.1
(iii), we can find progressively measurable maps
\bar{\eta}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to E
and
\bar{\zeta}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to E
with

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Theta}\int^{T}_{0}|\bar{\eta}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\nu(\mathrm{d}\vartheta)\right]+\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{\Theta}\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}\int^{T}_{0}|\bar{\zeta}_{t}(s,\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s\,\nu(\mathrm{d}\vartheta)\right]<\infty

such that, for
\nu
-a.e.
\vartheta\in\Theta
,

\begin{split}&\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta)=\mathbb{E}\big[\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta)\big]+\int^{T}_{0}\bar{\eta}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}\ \ \text{a.s.}\ \ \text{and}\\
&\bar{g}_{s}(\vartheta)=\mathbb{E}\big[\bar{g}_{s}(\vartheta)\big]+\int^{T}_{0}\bar{\zeta}_{t}(s,\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}\ \ \text{a.s.\ for a.e.\ $s\in[0,T]$.}\end{split}

(5.16)

Hence, there exist jointly measurable versions (still denoted by
\bar{\varphi},\bar{g},\bar{\eta},\bar{\zeta}
) such that the representation formula (
5.16
) and the integrability condition

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[|\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta)|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}|\bar{g}_{s}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{0}|\bar{\eta}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-s)^{\alpha}\int^{T}_{0}|\bar{\zeta}_{t}(s,\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathrm{d}s\right]<\infty\end{split}

(5.17)

hold for any
\vartheta\in\Theta
. Notice that the progressive measurability of
(\bar{g}_{s}(\vartheta))_{s\in[0,T]}
implies
\bar{\zeta}_{t}(s,\vartheta)=0
a.s. for a.e.
t>s
for any
\vartheta\in\Theta
. Define
\bar{p},\bar{q}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta\to E
by

\displaystyle\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta):=\mathbb{E}_{t}\left[e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)(T-t)}\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)(s-t)}\bar{g}_{s}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}s\right],

\displaystyle\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta):=e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)(T-t)}\bar{\eta}_{t}(\vartheta)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)(s-t)}\bar{\zeta}_{t}(s,\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}s,

for
(\omega,t,\vartheta)\in\Omega\times[0,T]\times\Theta
. Here, recall that
\mathbb{E}_{t}[\cdot]=\mathbb{E}[\cdot|\mathcal{F}_{t}]
denotes the conditional expectation operator with respect to
\mathcal{F}_{t}
. Clearly,
\bar{p}
and
\bar{q}
are progressively measurable. Furthermore, for any
\vartheta\in\Theta
,
\bar{p}(\vartheta)=(\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta))_{t\in[0,T]}
is an
E
-valued continuous semimartingale with
\bar{p}_{T}(\vartheta)=\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta)
a.s. In particular,
[\bar{p}_{T}(\cdot)]=\Phi
a.s. From the integrability condition (
5.17
) and
\alpha\in[0,1)
, one can easily check that

\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}|\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\left(\int^{T}_{0}|\bar{g}_{t}(\vartheta)|\,\mathrm{d}t\right)^{2}\right]<\infty\ \ \text{for any $\vartheta\in\Theta$.}

Let
\vartheta\in\Theta
be fixed. Observe that

\displaystyle e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)t}\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)-\bar{p}_{0}(\vartheta)+\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)s}\bar{g}_{s}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)T}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta)\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta)\big]\right\}+\int^{T}_{0}e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)s}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[\bar{g}_{s}(\vartheta)\big]-\mathbb{E}\big[\bar{g}_{s}(\vartheta)\big]\right\}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)T}\int^{t}_{0}\bar{\eta}_{s}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}+\int^{T}_{0}e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)s}\int^{s\wedge t}_{0}\bar{\zeta}_{r}(s,\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{r}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)s}\left\{e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)(T-s)}\bar{\eta}_{s}(\vartheta)+\int^{T}_{s}e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)(r-s)}\bar{\zeta}_{s}(r,\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}r\right\}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}

\displaystyle=\int^{t}_{0}e^{-\varpi(\vartheta)s}\bar{q}_{s}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\ \ \text{a.s.\ for any $t\in[0,T]$,}

(5.18)

where we used the representation formula (
5.16
) in the second equality and the stochastic Fubini theorem in the third equality. The application of the stochastic Fubini theorem can be easily justified by noting the progressive measurability and the integrability (
5.17
) of the map
(\omega,r,s)\mapsto\bar{\zeta}_{r}(s,\vartheta)
. By (
5.18
), Itô’s formula yields that the
E
-valued continuous semimartingale
\bar{p}(\vartheta)=(\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta))_{t\in[0,T]}
satisfies

\mathrm{d}\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)=\varpi(\vartheta)\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)-\bar{g}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t+\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ t\in[0,T].

Fix a constant
\lambda\geq 1
. Observe that, for any
\vartheta\in\Theta
, the pair
((e^{\lambda t}\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta))_{t\in[0,T]},(e^{\lambda t}\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta))_{t\in[0,T]})
is an adapted solution of the BSDE (
5.2
) on
E
with

\mathfrak{h}:=e^{\lambda T}\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta),\ \ \mathfrak{g}_{t}:=e^{\lambda t}\bar{g}_{t}(\vartheta),\ \ \text{and}\ \ \kappa=\lambda+\varpi(\vartheta).

Hence, by
Lemma

5.1
, we have

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}+(\lambda+\varpi(\vartheta))\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 28.45274pt+(\lambda+\varpi(\vartheta))^{\alpha}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 28.45274pt+(\lambda+\varpi(\vartheta))^{1+\alpha}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+(\lambda+\varpi(\vartheta))^{\alpha}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{2\lambda T}|\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta)|^{2}+\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{(\lambda+\varpi(\vartheta))^{1-\alpha}}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{g}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right],

where
C_{0}>0
is the constant appearing in
Lemma

5.1
. Noting that
\lambda\geq 1
,
\varpi(\vartheta)\geq 0
and
\alpha\in[0,1)
, we obtain

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}+(1+\varpi(\vartheta))\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 28.45274pt+(1+\varpi(\vartheta))^{\alpha}\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 28.45274pt+(1+\varpi(\vartheta))^{1+\alpha}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{p}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+(1+\varpi(\vartheta))^{\alpha}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{q}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{2\lambda T}|\bar{\varphi}(\vartheta)|^{2}+\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}|\bar{g}_{t}(\vartheta)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right].

Integrating both sides above by
\nu
with respect to
\vartheta\in\Theta
and using Tonelli’s theorem (recall that the measure
\nu
is
\sigma
-finite by the assumption), we get

\begin{split}&\mathbb{E}\left[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{2\lambda t}\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}\|\bar{q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right.\\
&\left.\hskip 28.45274pt+\sup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\bar{q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]\\
&\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{2\lambda T}\|\bar{\varphi}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}+\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\bar{g}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right].\end{split}

(5.19)

The last line above is finite thanks to the assumption (
4.37
) (with
\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})=\mathcal{G}_{t}
). Now we define
\mathcal{P}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}
and
\mathcal{Q}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
by

\mathcal{P}_{t}:=\begin{cases}[\bar{p}_{t}(\cdot)]\ \ &\text{if $\|\bar{p}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}<\infty$,}\\
0\ \ &\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}\ \ \text{and}\ \ \mathcal{Q}_{t}:=\begin{cases}[\bar{q}_{t}(\cdot)]\ \ &\text{if $\|\bar{q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}<\infty$,}\\
0\ \ &\text{otherwise,}\end{cases}

where
[\psi(\cdot)]
denotes the
\nu
-equivalence class of a measurable function
\psi:\Theta\to E
. By
Lemma

A.1
(i), we see that
\mathcal{P}
and
\mathcal{Q}
are (
\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}
- and
\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
-valued, respectively) progressively measurable processes. The estimate (
5.19
) implies that
\mathcal{P}_{t}=[\bar{p}_{t}(\cdot)]
and
\mathcal{Q}_{t}=[\bar{q}_{t}(\cdot)]
a.s. for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
and that
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
satisfies (
5.15
). By the construction,
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
is the solution of the BSEE (
5.14
) with the good representative
(\bar{p},\bar{q},\bar{g})
. This completes the proof.
∎

Now we can prove
Theorem

4.8
.

Proof of
Theorem

4.8
.

Let
Assumption

3
hold. We denote by
\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
the set of all pairs
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
of the
\mathbb{P}\otimes\mathrm{d}t
-equivalence classes of progressively measurable processes
\mathcal{P}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}
and
\mathcal{Q}:\Omega\times[0,T]\to\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}
such that

\|(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{Q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2}<\infty.

Then,
(\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha},\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}})
is a Banach space. Furthermore, for each
\lambda\geq 1
, define

\|(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha,\lambda}}:=\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{Q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]^{1/2},\ \ (\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})\in\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}.

Clearly,
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha,\lambda}}
is another norm on
\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
and equivalent to the original one
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}}
.

By
Lemma

5.3
, every solution of the BSEE (
4.35
) satisfying the integrability condition (
4.40
) must belong to the space
\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
. Take an arbitrary
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})\in\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
. By
Assumption

3
, we see that

\mathbb{E}\left[\|\Phi\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}\|\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}_{t},\mathcal{Q}_{t})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]<\infty.

Hence, by
Lemma

5.3
, there exists a unique solution
(\tilde{\mathcal{P}},\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})\in\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
to the BSEE

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{t}(\vartheta)=\varpi(\vartheta)\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t-\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}_{t},\mathcal{Q}_{t})(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t+\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}_{t}(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ \vartheta\in\Theta,\ t\in[0,T],\\
\tilde{\mathcal{P}}_{T}(\vartheta)=\Phi(\vartheta),\ \ \vartheta\in\Theta.\end{cases}

Set
\Xi_{T,\alpha}(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}):=(\tilde{\mathcal{P}},\tilde{\mathcal{Q}})
. Notice that an element
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})\in\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
is a solution to the BSEE (
4.35
) if and only if it is a fixed point of the map
\Xi_{T,\alpha}:\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}\to\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
. In the following, we will show that the map
\Xi_{T,\alpha}
is contractive on the Banach space
\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
under the equivalent norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha,\lambda}}
for a sufficiently large
\lambda\geq 1
, showing the existence and uniqueness of the fixed point.

Fix
\lambda\geq 1
, which will be determined later. Let
(\mathcal{P}^{1},\mathcal{Q}^{1})
and
(\mathcal{P}^{2},\mathcal{Q}^{2})
be two elements of
\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
. Define
(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{i},\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{i}):=\Xi_{T,\alpha}(\mathcal{P}^{i},\mathcal{Q}^{i})
for
i=1,2
. Then
(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{2},\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{2})\in\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
is the unique solution to the BSEE

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{t}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{2}_{t})(\vartheta)=\varpi(\vartheta)(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{t}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{2}_{t})(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t-\big(\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}^{1}_{t},\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{t})-\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}^{2}_{t},\mathcal{Q}^{2}_{t})\big)(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}t+(\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{1}_{t}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{2}_{t})(\vartheta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\\
\hskip 170.71652pt\vartheta\in\Theta,\ t\in[0,T],\\
(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{1}_{T}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{2}_{T})(\vartheta)=0,\ \ \vartheta\in\Theta.\end{cases}

Hence, by the estimate (
5.15
) in
Lemma

5.3
, it holds that

\big\|(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{2},\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{2})\big\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha,\lambda}}^{2}\leq C_{0}\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}^{1}_{t},\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{t})-\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}^{2}_{t},\mathcal{Q}^{2}_{t})\|^{2}_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}\,\mathrm{d}t\right],

where
C_{0}>0
is the constant appearing in
Lemma

5.1
. By the assumption (
4.38
), we see that

\displaystyle\big\|(\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{P}}^{2},\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{1}-\tilde{\mathcal{Q}}^{2})\big\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha,\lambda}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}\mathbb{E}\left[\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}2L^{2}\Big\{\|\mathcal{P}^{1}_{t}-\mathcal{P}^{2}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}+\|\mathcal{Q}^{1}_{t}-\mathcal{Q}^{2}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle=2C_{0}L^{2}\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}\big\|(\mathcal{P}^{1}-\mathcal{P}^{2},\mathcal{Q}^{1}-\mathcal{Q}^{2})\big\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha,\lambda}}^{2}.

Therefore, noting that
\alpha\in[0,1)
and taking
\lambda\geq 1
(depending only on
L
and
\alpha
) such that
2C_{0}L^{2}\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}<1
, the map
\Xi_{T,\alpha}
becomes contractive on the Banach space
\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
under the norm
\|\cdot\|_{\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha,\lambda}}
. Hence, by Banach’s fixed point theorem, the map
\Xi_{T,\alpha}:\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}\to\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
has a unique fixed point. This means that the BSEE (
4.35
) has a unique solution satisfying (
4.40
) and that the solution belongs to
\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
.

Next, we show the estimate (
4.39
). Let
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
be the solution to the BSEE (
4.35
) satisfying (
4.40
). Again by the estimate (
5.15
) in
Lemma

5.3
and the assumption (
4.38
), it holds that

\displaystyle\mathbb{E}\left[\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{Q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 28.45274pt+\esssup_{t\in[0,T]}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t+\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{Q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{2\lambda T}\|\Phi\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}+\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}\|\mathcal{G}(t,\mathcal{P}_{t},\mathcal{Q}_{t})\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\right]

\displaystyle\leq C_{0}\mathbb{E}\left[e^{2\lambda T}\|\Phi\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}+\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}\int^{T}_{0}(T-t)^{\alpha}e^{2\lambda t}3\big\{\|\mathcal{G}(t,0,0)\|_{\mathbb{H}_{0}}^{2}+L^{2}\|\mathcal{P}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{1+\alpha}}^{2}+L^{2}\|\mathcal{Q}_{t}\|_{\mathbb{H}_{\alpha}}^{2}\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t\right].

Hence, noting that
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})\in\mathbb{S}_{T,\alpha}
, taking
\lambda\geq 1
(depending only on
L
and
\alpha
) such that
3C_{0}L^{2}\frac{\Gamma(1-\alpha)}{\lambda^{1-\alpha}}\leq\frac{1}{2}
, we obtain the desired estimate (
4.39
). This completes the proof.
∎

Appendix

Appendix A
Remarks on measurability issues

In the study of BSEEs introduced in this paper, we sometimes identify a function-space-valued measurable map with a jointly measurable multi-variate map. Let us make remarks on the measurability issues for the identification of these two objectives.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
(A,\mathcal{A})
be a measurable space, and let
(B,\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{m})
be a countably generated
\sigma
-finite measure space. Denote by
(L^{2}(B;E),\|\cdot\|_{L^{2}(B;E)},\langle\cdot,\cdot\rangle_{L^{2}(B;E)})
the separable Hilbert space of equivalence classes of square-integrable measurable functions from
(B,\mathcal{B},\mathfrak{m})
to a Euclidean space
E
. For each measurable function
\psi:B\to E
, we denote its
\mathfrak{m}
-equivalence class by
[\psi]
.

(i)

Let
\bar{f}:A\times B\to E
be a jointly measurable map (that is,
\mathcal{A}\otimes\mathcal{B}
-measurable) such that
\int_{B}|\bar{f}(a,b)|^{2}\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)<\infty
for each
a\in A
. Define
f(a):=[\bar{f}(a,\cdot)]
. Then, the map
a\mapsto f(a)
is a (strongly) measurable map from
(A,\mathcal{A})
to
L^{2}(B;E)
equipped with its Borel
\sigma
-field.

(ii)

Let
f:A\to L^{2}(B;E)
be a (strongly) measurable map. Then, there exists a jointly measurable map
\bar{f}:A\times B\to E
such that
f(a)=[\bar{f}(a,\cdot)]
for any
a\in A
.

(iii)

Let
\bar{\varphi}:\Omega\times B\to E
be an
\mathcal{F}_{T}\otimes\mathcal{B}
-measurable map such that
\mathbb{E}[\int_{B}|\bar{\varphi}(b)|^{2}\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)]<\infty
. Then, there exists a progressively measurable map
\bar{\zeta}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times B\to E
such that

\mathbb{E}\left[\int_{B}\int^{T}_{0}|\bar{\zeta}_{t}(b)|^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)\right]<\infty

(A.1)

and

\bar{\varphi}(b)=\mathbb{E}[\bar{\varphi}(b)]+\int^{T}_{0}\bar{\zeta}_{t}(b)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}\ \ \text{a.s.\ for $\mathfrak{m}$-a.e.\ $b\in B$.}

(A.2)

Proof.

(i)

For any
\eta\in L^{2}(B;E)
(with a measurable representative
\bar{\eta}:B\to E
), Fubini’s theorem shows that
A\ni a\mapsto\langle\eta,f(a)\rangle_{L^{2}(B;E)}=\int_{B}\langle\bar{\eta}(b),\bar{f}(a,b)\rangle\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)\in\mathbb{R}
is measurable, which means that
a\mapsto f(a)
is weakly measurable and equivalently (thanks to the separability of
L^{2}(B;E)
) strongly measurable.

(ii)

Let
\{x_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
be a countable dense subset of
L^{2}(B;E)
(with measurable representatives
\bar{x}_{k}:B\to E
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
). Define a map
\bar{f}_{n}:A\times B\to E
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
by

\bar{f}_{n}(a,b):=\sum_{k\in\mathbb{N}}\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{A^{n}_{k}}(a)\bar{x}_{k}(b),\ \ (a,b)\in A\times B.

Here,
A^{n}_{k}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}
, are defined inductively by
A^{n}_{1}:=f^{-1}(B(x_{1},2^{-n}))
and
A^{n}_{k+1}:=f^{-1}(B(x_{k+1},2^{-n}))\setminus(\bigcup^{k}_{j=1}A^{n}_{j})
for
k\in\mathbb{N}
, where
B(x,r)
denotes the open ball in
L^{2}(B;E)
with center
x\in L^{2}(B;E)
and radius
r>0
. Since
f:A\to L^{2}(B;E)
is strongly measurable, we see that
A^{n}_{k}\in\mathcal{A}
for any
n,k\in\mathbb{N}
, and
\{A^{n}_{k}\}_{k\in\mathbb{N}}
is a disjoint family covering
A
for each
n\in\mathbb{N}
. Hence, the map
\bar{f}_{n}:A\times B\to E
is well-defined and jointly measurable. Furthermore,
\|f(a)-[\bar{f}_{n}(a,\cdot)]\|_{L^{2}(B;E)}\leq 2^{-n}
for any
a\in A
and
n\in\mathbb{N}
. By Minkowski’s integral inequality, the above implies that the function
b\mapsto F(a,b):=\sum^{\infty}_{n=1}|\bar{f}_{n+1}(a,b)-\bar{f}_{n}(a,b)|
is square integrable with respect to the measure
\mathfrak{m}
for each
a\in A
. Now we set
\bar{f}(a,b):=\lim_{n\to\infty}\bar{f}_{n}(a,b)
if the limit exists in
E
and
\bar{f}(a,b):=0
otherwise. Then
\bar{f}:A\times B\to E
is jointly measurable and such that
\bar{f}_{n}(a,b)\to\bar{f}(a,b)
as
n\to\infty
for
\mathfrak{m}
-a.e.
b\in B
for any
a\in A
. Noting that
|\bar{f}_{n}(a,b)|\leq|\bar{f}_{1}(a,b)|+F(a,b)
, by the dominated convergence theorem,
[\bar{f}_{n}(a,\cdot)]\to[\bar{f}(a,\cdot)]
in
L^{2}(B;E)
as
n\to\infty
for any
a\in A
. Hence, it holds that
f(a)=[\bar{f}(a,\cdot)]
in
L^{2}(B;E)
for any
a\in A
.

(iii)

Without loss of generality, we may assume that
\mathbb{E}[\bar{\varphi}(b)]=0
for any
b\in B
. Define
\varphi(\omega):=[\bar{\varphi}(\omega,\cdot)]
if
\int_{B}|\bar{\varphi}(\omega,b)|^{2}\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)<\infty
and
\varphi(\omega):=0
otherwise. By the assertion (i), together with the assumption that
\mathbb{E}[\int_{B}|\bar{\varphi}(b)|^{2}\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)]<\infty
, we see that
\varphi:\Omega\to L^{2}(B;E)
is strongly
\mathcal{F}_{T}
-measurable and
\mathbb{E}[\|\varphi\|_{L^{2}(B;E)}^{2}]<\infty
. Hence, by the martingale representation theorem (applying to the
L^{2}(B;E)
-valued random variable
\varphi
), there exists a unique progressively measurable process
\zeta:\Omega\times[0,T]\to L^{2}(B;E)
such that
\mathbb{E}[\int^{T}_{0}\|\zeta_{t}\|_{L^{2}(B;E)}^{2}\,\mathrm{d}t]<\infty
and
\varphi=\int^{T}_{0}\zeta_{t}\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}
in
L^{2}(B;E)
a.s. By the assertion (ii), there exists a (jointly) progressively measurable map
\bar{\zeta}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times B\to E
such that
[\bar{\zeta}_{t}(\omega,\cdot)]=\zeta_{t}(\omega)
. Clearly, the integrability condition (
A.1
) holds. Furthermore, for any
\eta\in L^{2}(B;E)
with a measurable representative
\bar{\eta}:B\to E
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{B}\langle\bar{\eta}(b),\bar{\varphi}(b)\rangle\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)

\displaystyle=\langle\eta,\varphi\rangle_{L^{2}(B;E)}=\left\langle\eta,\int^{T}_{0}\zeta_{t}\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}\right\rangle_{L^{2}(B;E)}=\int^{T}_{0}\langle\eta,\zeta_{t}\rangle_{L^{2}(B;E)}\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}

\displaystyle=\int^{T}_{0}\int_{B}\langle\bar{\eta}(b),\bar{\zeta}_{t}(b)\rangle\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}=\int_{B}\left\langle\bar{\eta}(b),\int^{T}_{0}\bar{\zeta}_{t}(b)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}\right\rangle\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)\ \ \text{a.s.},

where we used the stochastic Fubini theorem (cf.
[
33
, Theorem 2.2]
) in the last equality. Since
L^{2}(B;E)
is separable, there exists a
\mathbb{P}
-null set
\mathcal{N}\in\mathcal{F}
(independent of the choice of
\eta
) such that the equality
\int_{B}\langle\bar{\eta}(b),\bar{\varphi}(b,\omega)\rangle\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)=\int_{B}\langle\bar{\eta}(b),(\int^{T}_{0}\bar{\zeta}_{t}(b)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t})(\omega)\rangle\,\mathfrak{m}(\mathrm{d}b)
holds for any
\eta=[\bar{\eta}]\in L^{2}(B;E)
and any
\omega\in\Omega\setminus\mathcal{N}
. Hence, the equality (
A.2
) holds. This completes the proof.

∎

Appendix B
Relationships between BSEEs and BSVIEs

In the study of the global maximum principle for SVEs, Wang and Yong
[
35
]
considered (non-convolution type) SVEs with regular kernels. They introduced first and second order adjoint equations in form of the so-called
backward stochastic Volterra integral equations
(BSVIEs). In this section, we investigate a relationship between our adjoint equations (
4.31
) and (
4.32
) in form of BSEEs and that of
[
35
]
in form of BSVIEs.

Suppose that
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

2
hold. Fix a control process
\hat{u}\in\mathcal{U}
. In this setting, the first order adjoint equation in
[
35
]
can be written as the following (Type-II) BSVIE
3
3

3

Here, we use a slightly different but equivalent formulation for the Type-II BSVIE from
[
35
]
.
:

\begin{cases}p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)=-\hat{h}_{x}^{\top}-\int^{T}_{t}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(s)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t)=-\hat{f}_{x}(t)^{\top}+\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{b}(T-t)^{\top}p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)\\
\hskip 56.9055pt+\int^{T}_{t}\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}[p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s.\ \ t\in[0,T].\end{cases}

(B.1)

Here and below, the super script “
\mathrm{V}
” represents “Volterra”. A tuple

(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2})=\Big((p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t))_{0\leq t\leq T},(q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t))_{0\leq t\leq T},(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t))_{0\leq t\leq T},(q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t))_{0\leq t\leq s\leq T}\Big)

is said to be an
adapted M-solution
to the BSVIE (
B.1
) if the maps

\displaystyle\Omega\times[0,T]\ni(\omega,t)\mapsto p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t),q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t),p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}\ \ \text{and}

\displaystyle\Omega\times[0,T]\times[0,T]\ni(\omega,t,s)\mapsto q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[0,s]}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n}

are progressively measurable, square-integrable and satisfy (
B.1
), together with the constraint

p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)=\mathbb{E}\big[p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]+\int^{s}_{0}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ s\in[0,T].

(B.2)

Notice that
(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1})
is determined by the first line in (
B.1
);
p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)=-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\hat{h}_{x}]^{\top}
, and
q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}
is the martingale integrand of the
\mathbb{R}^{n}
-valued martingale
p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}
defined via the martingale representation theorem:

p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(s)=p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(0)+\int^{s}_{0}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ s\in[0,T].

(B.3)

Under suitable integrability conditions on the kernels
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
, the BSVIE (
B.1
) admits a unique adapted M-solution; see
[
38
]
.

The second order adjoint equation introduced in
[
35
]
is written as the following system of BSVIEs:

\begin{cases}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)=-\hat{h}_{xx}-\int^{T}_{t}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(s)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t)=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{b}(T-t)^{\top}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)\\
\hskip 56.9055pt+\int^{T}_{t}\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(t)=-\hat{f}_{xx}(t)+\big\langle K_{b}(T-t)^{\top}p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t),\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t),\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle\\
\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{T}_{t}\Big\{\big\langle K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big],\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t),\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\hskip 14.22636pt+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)K_{\sigma}(T-t)\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\\
\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{T}_{t}\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\Big\{K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]^{\top}K_{\sigma}(s-t)+K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]K_{\sigma}(T-t)\Big\}\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{T}_{t}\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s)\big]K_{\sigma}(s-t)\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{T}_{t}\int^{T}_{s}\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\Big\{K_{\sigma}(r-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]^{\top}K_{\sigma}(s-t)+K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]K_{\sigma}(r-t)\Big\}\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\,\mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}s,\\
\hskip 199.16928ptt\in[0,T],\\
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,t)=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{b}(T-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r)\big]^{\top}+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r,t)^{\top}\\
\hskip 56.9055pt+\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{b}(r-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r)\big]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(r-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r,t)\\
\hskip 56.9055pt+\int^{T}_{r}\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r)\big]^{\top}+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r,t)^{\top}\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
\hskip 56.9055pt+\int^{r}_{t}\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ 0\leq t\leq r\leq T.\end{cases}

(B.4)

A tuple

\displaystyle(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4})

\displaystyle=\Big((P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t))_{0\leq t\leq T},(Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t))_{0\leq t\leq T},(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t))_{0\leq t\leq T},(Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t))_{0\leq t\leq s\leq T},

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(t))_{0\leq t\leq T},(Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s,t))_{0\leq t\leq s\leq T},(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,t))_{0\leq t\leq r\leq T},(Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t))_{0\leq t\leq s\leq r\leq T}\Big)

is said to be an adapted M-solution to the BSVIE system (
B.4
) if the maps

\displaystyle\Omega\times[0,T]\ni(\omega,t)\mapsto P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t),Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t),P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t),P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},

\displaystyle\Omega\times[0,T]\times[0,T]\ni(\omega,t,s)\mapsto Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[0,s]}(t),Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s,t)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[0,s]}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},

\displaystyle\Omega\times[0,T]\times[0,T]\ni(\omega,t,r)\mapsto P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,t)_{[0,r]}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n},\ \ \text{and}

\displaystyle\Omega\times[0,T]\times[0,T]\times[0,T]\ni(\omega,t,s,r)\mapsto Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[0,r]}(s)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[0,s]}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}

are progressively measurable, square-integrable and satisfy (
B.4
), together with the constraints

\begin{cases}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)=\mathbb{E}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]+\int^{s}_{0}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ 0\leq s\leq T,\\
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s)=\mathbb{E}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s)\big]+\int^{s}_{0}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ 0\leq s\leq T,\\
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)=\mathbb{E}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]+\int^{s}_{0}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ 0\leq s\leq r\leq T.\end{cases}

(B.5)

Notice that the pair
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1})
is determined by the first line of (
B.4
);
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)=-\mathbb{E}_{t}[\hat{h}_{xx}]
, and
Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}
is the martingale integrand of the
\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
-valued martingale
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}
defined via the martingale representation theorem:

P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(s)=P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(0)+\int^{s}_{0}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ s\in[0,T].

(B.6)

By
[
35
, Theorem 5.1]
, when
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
are bounded, the BSVIE system (
B.4
) has a unique adapted M-solution.

The following proposition shows relationships between BSEEs and BSVIEs.

Proposition B.1
.

Suppose that
Assumption

1
and
Assumption

2
with
\alpha=0
hold. Fix a control process
\hat{u}\in\mathcal{U}
.

(1-A)

If
(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2})
is the adapted M-solution of the BSVIE (
B.1
), then the pair
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
of the
\mu
-equivalence classes of progressively measurable maps
\hat{p},\hat{q}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{n}
defined by

\begin{split}&\hat{p}_{t}(\theta):=e^{-\theta(T-t)}p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s,\\
&\hat{q}_{t}(\theta):=e^{-\theta(T-t)}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ (\omega,t,\theta)\in\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+},\end{split}

(B.7)

is the solution of the BSEE (
4.31
).

(1-B)

Conversely, if
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
is the solution of the BSEE (
4.31
), then the tuple
(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2})
of progressively measurable maps defined by

\begin{split}&p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t):=-\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[\hat{h}_{x}\big]^{\top},\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
&p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t):=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}]-\hat{f}_{x}(t)^{\top},\ \ t\in[0,T],\end{split}

(B.8)

together with the martingale representations (
B.2
) and (
B.3
), is the adapted M-solution of the BSVIE (
B.1
).

(2-A)

Assume that
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4})
is the adapted M-solution of the BSVIE system (
B.4
). Then, the following hold:

(i)

The pair
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
of the
\mu^{\otimes 2}
-equivalence classes of progressively measurable maps
\hat{P},\hat{Q}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
defined by

\begin{split}&\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\\
&:=e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(T-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pte^{-\theta_{1}(T-t)}\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}s+e^{-\theta_{2}(T-t)}\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{1}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(r-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r)\big]\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{T}_{t}\left\{e^{-\theta_{1}(r-t)}\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}s+e^{-\theta_{2}(r-t)}\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta_{1}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s\right\}\,\mathrm{d}r,\\
&\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\\
&:=e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(T-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+e^{-\theta_{1}(T-t)}\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(s-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}s+e^{-\theta_{2}(T-t)}\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{1}(s-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(r-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r,t)\,\mathrm{d}r\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{T}_{t}\left\{e^{-\theta_{1}(r-t)}\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(s-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}s+e^{-\theta_{2}(r-t)}\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta_{1}(s-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\mathrm{d}s\right\}\,\mathrm{d}r,\\
&\hskip 170.71652pt(\omega,t,\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2},\end{split}

(B.9)

is the solution of the BSEE (
4.32
).

(ii)

The pair
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
of the
\mu
-equivalence classes of progressively measurable maps
\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
defined by

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{t}(\theta):=e^{-\theta(T-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathcal{Q}_{t}(\theta):=e^{-\theta(T-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ (\omega,t,\theta)\in\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+},

is the solution of the following BSEE:

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\mathcal{P}_{t}(\theta)=\theta\mathcal{P}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t-\big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathcal{P}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathcal{Q}_{t}]\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathcal{Q}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\ t\in[0,T],\\
\mathcal{P}_{T}(\theta)=-\hat{h}_{xx},\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.\end{cases}

(B.10)

(iii)

For each
r\in[0,T]
, the pair
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
of the
\mu
-equivalence classes of progressively measurable maps
\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r}:\Omega\times[0,r]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
defined by

\begin{split}&\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta):=e^{-\theta(T-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r)\big]^{\top}+e^{-\theta(r-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r)\big]+\int^{T}_{r}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r)\big]^{\top}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s,\\
&\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}(\theta):=e^{-\theta(T-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r,t)^{\top}+e^{-\theta(r-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r,t)+\int^{T}_{r}e^{-\theta(s-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r,t)^{\top}\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s,\ \ \ (\omega,t,\theta)\in\Omega\times[0,r]\times\mathbb{R}_{+},\end{split}

(B.11)

is the solution of the following BSEE on
[0,r]
:

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta)=\theta\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t-\big\{\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}]\big\}\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\\
\hskip 199.16928pt\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\ t\in[0,r],\\
\mathscr{P}^{r}_{r}(\theta)=\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{r}],\hat{b}_{xx}(r)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{r}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(r)\big\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(r)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(r)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{r}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(r)\\
\hskip 85.35826pt+\mu[\hat{P}_{r}(\theta,\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(r)+\mu[\hat{Q}_{r}(\theta,\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(r),\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.\end{cases}

(B.12)

(2-B)

Conversely, assume that
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
is the solution of the BSEE (
4.32
), that
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
is the solution of the BSEE (
B.10
), and that
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
is the solution of the BSEE (
B.12
) for each
r\in[0,T]
such that
(\omega,t,\theta,r)\mapsto\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[0,r]}(t),\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}(\theta)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[0,r]}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
are progressively measurable. Then, the tuple
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4})
of progressively measurable maps defined by

\begin{split}&P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t):=-\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[\hat{h}_{xx}\big],\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
&P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t):=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathcal{P}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathcal{Q}_{t}],\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
&P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(t):=\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\big\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(t)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t),\ \ t\in[0,T],\\
&P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,t):=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}],\ \ 0\leq t\leq r\leq T,\end{split}

(B.13)

together with the martingale representations (
B.5
) and (
B.6
), is the adapted M-solution of the BSVIE system (
B.4
).

Remark B.2
.

The assumption that (
4.3
) holds with
\alpha=0
is required in order to ensure the square-integrability condition (with respect to the time parameter) for the adapted M-solution of the BSVIE system (
B.4
). Actually,
Proposition

B.1
holds true even in the general (singular) case with
\alpha\in(0,1)
if we replaced the square-integrability requirement in the definition of the adapted M-solution of the BSVIEs to a suitable “weighted square-integrability condition” in a similar manner as in the estimate (
4.39
). However, since the main finding in
Proposition

B.1
is the structural relationships between BSEEs and BSVIEs, we do not state such generalization here for the sake of simplicity of presentation.

Proof of
Proposition

B.1
.

In this proof, we use the (stochastic) Fubini theorem several times. The required integrability conditions for the applications of the (stochastic) Fubini theorem can be easily checked noting the assumption that (
4.3
) holds with
\alpha=0
, and we omit the proofs of them. Instead, we focus on the structural relationships.

(1-A): From
(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2})
to
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
.
Let
(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2})
be the adapted M-solution of the BSVIE (
B.1
), and define
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
by (
B.7
). Noting
p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)=\mathbb{E}_{t}[p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(T)]
and the martingale representations (
B.2
) and (
B.3
), by the same argument as in the proof of
Lemma

5.3
, we see that
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
is the solution to the following BSEE:

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)=\theta\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t-p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t)\,\mathrm{d}t+\hat{q}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\ t\in[0,T],\\
\hat{p}_{T}(\theta)=p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(T),\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.\end{cases}

(B.14)

Hence, noting that
p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(T)=-\hat{h}_{x}^{\top}
, in order to show that
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
is the solution to the BSEE (
4.31
), it suffices to show that

p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t)=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}]-\hat{f}_{x}(t)^{\top}.

(B.15)

However, thanks to the representation formulas (
4.1
) for the kernels
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
, together with the definitions (
B.7
) of
\hat{p}
and
\hat{q}
, using Fubini’s theorem, we have

\begin{split}&\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}]=K_{b}(T-t)^{\top}p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s,\\
&\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}]=K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(B.16)

From these expressions, together with the second equation in the BSVIE (
B.1
), we see that (
B.15
) holds. Hence,
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
is the solution of the BSEE (
4.31
).

(1-B): From
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
to
(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2})
.
Let
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
be the solution of the BSEE (
4.31
). Define
(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2})
by (
B.8
), together with the martingale representations (
B.2
) and (
B.3
). By the expression (
B.8
), the BSEE (
4.31
) for
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
can be written as (
B.14
). Using Itô’s formula, for each
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\hat{p}_{t}(\theta)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\hat{q}_{s}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}

\displaystyle=e^{-\theta(T-t)}p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(T)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=e^{-\theta(T-t)}\left\{p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(s)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}\right\}+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\left\{\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]+\int^{s}_{t}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,r)\,\mathrm{d}W_{r}\right\}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=e^{-\theta(T-t)}p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\left\{e^{-\theta(T-s)}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(s)+\int^{T}_{s}e^{-\theta(r-s)}q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r,s)\,\mathrm{d}r\right\}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},

where we used the martingale representations (
B.2
) and (
B.3
) in the third equality and the stochastic Fubini theorem in the fourth equality. From this, we get the equalities in form of (
B.7
). Hence, as before, thanks to the representation formulas (
4.1
) for the kernels
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
, using Fubini’s theorem, we get (
B.16
). Inserting the expressions in (
B.16
) to the definition (
B.8
) of
p_{2}^{\mathrm{V}}
, we see that
(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2})
is the adapted M-solution of the BSVIE (
B.1
).

(2-A): From
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4})
to
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
,
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
and
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
.
Suppose that we are given the adapted M-solution
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4})
of the BSVIE system (
B.4
).

First, we show the assertion (i) in (2-A). Define
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
by (
B.9
). Notice that

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(T-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(T)\big]+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[G^{\mathrm{V}}_{s}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\big]\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(T-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(s-t)}Z^{\mathrm{V}}_{t}(s,\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}s,

where

\displaystyle G^{\mathrm{V}}_{s}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})

\displaystyle=e^{-\theta_{1}(T-s)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)^{\top}+e^{-\theta_{2}(T-s)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)+P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int^{T}_{s}e^{-\theta_{1}(r-s)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int^{T}_{s}e^{-\theta_{2}(r-s)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\,\mathrm{d}r

and

\displaystyle Z^{\mathrm{V}}_{t}(s,\theta_{1},\theta_{2})

\displaystyle=e^{-\theta_{1}(T-s)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)^{\top}+e^{-\theta_{2}(T-s)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)+Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s,t)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int^{T}_{s}e^{-\theta_{1}(r-s)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}r+\int^{T}_{s}e^{-\theta_{2}(r-s)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\,\mathrm{d}r.

By the martingale representations (
B.5
) and (
B.6
), together with the stochastic Fubini theorem, we see that

P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(T)=\mathbb{E}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(T)\big]+\int^{T}_{0}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}

and

G^{\mathrm{V}}_{s}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\mathbb{E}\big[G^{\mathrm{V}}_{s}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\big]+\int^{s}_{0}Z^{\mathrm{V}}_{t}(s,\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}.

Hence, by the same argument as in the proof of
Lemma

5.3
, we see that
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
is the solution to the following BSEE:

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t-G^{\mathrm{V}}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}t+\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ (\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2},\ t\in[0,T],\\
\hat{P}_{T}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(T),\ \ (\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}.\end{cases}

Therefore, noting that
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(T)=-\hat{h}_{xx}
, in order to show that
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
is the solution to the BSEE (
4.32
), it suffices to show that

\begin{split}G^{\mathrm{V}}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})&=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\mu[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(t)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\mu[\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)\\
&\hskip 14.22636pt+\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\rangle+\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(t).\end{split}

(B.17)

Thanks to the representation formulas (
4.1
) for
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
, together with the definitions (
B.9
) of
\hat{P}
and
\hat{Q}
, using Fubini’s theorem, we have

\displaystyle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]=e^{-\theta_{2}(T-t)}\left\{K_{b}(T-t)^{\top}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(r-t)}\left\{K_{b}(T-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r)\big]^{\top}+K_{b}(r-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r)\big]\vphantom{\int^{T}_{s}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 142.26378pt+\int^{r}_{t}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{r}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r)\big]^{\top}\mathrm{d}s\right\}\,\mathrm{d}r

and

\displaystyle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]=e^{-\theta_{2}(T-t)}\left\{K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s\right\}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(r-t)}\left\{K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r,t)^{\top}+K_{\sigma}(r-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r,t)\vphantom{\int^{T}_{s}}\right.

\displaystyle\left.\hskip 142.26378pt+\int^{r}_{t}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{r}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r,t)^{\top}\mathrm{d}s\right\}\,\mathrm{d}r.

From the above two expressions, together with the second and fourth equations in the BSVIE system (
B.4
), we see that

\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{t}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]=e^{-\theta_{2}(T-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(r-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,t)\,\mathrm{d}r.

(B.18)

By similar calculations, we can show that

\mu[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(t)+\mu[\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)=e^{-\theta_{1}(T-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(t)^{\top}+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{1}(r-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,t)^{\top}\mathrm{d}r.

(B.19)

Furthermore, again by the representation formula (
4.1
) for
K_{\sigma}
and the definition (
B.9
) of
\hat{P}
, using Fubini’s theorem, we have

\begin{split}&\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\\
&=K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)K_{\sigma}(T-t)\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\int^{T}_{t}\Big\{K_{\sigma}(T-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]^{\top}K_{\sigma}(s-t)+K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]K_{\sigma}(T-t)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
