&\hskip 28.45274pt+\int^{T}_{t}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s)\big]K_{\sigma}(s-t)\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\int^{T}_{t}\int^{T}_{s}\Big\{K_{\sigma}(r-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]^{\top}K_{\sigma}(s-t)+K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]K_{\sigma}(r-t)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}r\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(B.20)

From (
B.20
) and the relation (
B.16
) between
(\hat{p},\hat{q})
and
(p^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},p^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2})
, together with the third equation in the BSVIE system (
B.4
), we see that

\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{t}],\hat{b}_{xx}(t)\rangle+\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{t}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(t)\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(t)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{t}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)=P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(t).

(B.21)

By (
B.18
), (
B.19
) and (
B.21
), we obtain (
B.17
). Hence,
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
is the solution of the BSEE (
4.32
).

The assertion (ii) in (2-A) can be proved by the same argument as in (1-A) above, and we omit it.

Concerning with the assertion (iii) in (2-A), notice that
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
defined by (
B.11
) can be rewritten as

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta)=e^{-\theta(r-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[\varphi^{\mathrm{V}}(r,\theta)\big]+\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}(\theta)=e^{-\theta(r-t)}\eta^{\mathrm{V}}_{t}(r,\theta)+\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s,

where

\displaystyle\varphi^{\mathrm{V}}(r,\theta):=e^{-\theta(T-r)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r)^{\top}+P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r)+\int^{T}_{r}e^{-\theta(s-r)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r)^{\top}\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\eta^{\mathrm{V}}_{t}(r,\theta):=e^{-\theta(T-r)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r,t)^{\top}+Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r,t)+\int^{T}_{r}e^{-\theta(s-r)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r,t)^{\top}\mathrm{d}s.

By the martingale representations (
B.5
) and (
B.6
), together with the stochastic Fubini theorem, we see that

\varphi^{\mathrm{V}}(r,\theta)=\mathbb{E}\big[\varphi^{\mathrm{V}}(r,\theta)\big]+\int^{r}_{0}\eta^{\mathrm{V}}_{t}(r,\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)=\mathbb{E}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]+\int^{s}_{0}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t}.

Hence, by the same argument as in the proof of
Lemma

5.3
, we see that
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
is the solution to the following BSEE on
[0,r]
:

\begin{cases}\mathrm{d}\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta)=\theta\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}t-P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,t)\,\mathrm{d}t+\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{t},\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+},\ t\in[0,r],\\
\mathscr{P}^{r}_{r}(\theta)=\varphi^{\mathrm{V}}(r,\theta),\ \ \theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}.\end{cases}

Furthermore, by (
B.19
) and (
B.21
), we have

\displaystyle\varphi^{\mathrm{V}}(r,\theta)

\displaystyle=\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{r}],\hat{b}_{xx}(r)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{r}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(r)\big\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(r)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(r)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{r}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(r)

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\mu[\hat{P}_{r}(\theta,\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(r)+\mu[\hat{Q}_{r}(\theta,\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(r).

Hence, in order to show that
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
is the solution of the BSEE (
B.12
), it suffices to show that

P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,t)=\hat{b}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(t)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}].

(B.22)

However, thanks to the representation formulas (
4.1
) for the kernels
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
, together with the definitions (
B.11
) of
\mathscr{P}^{r}
and
\mathscr{Q}^{r}
, using Fubini’s theorem, we have

\begin{split}&\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}]=K_{b}(T-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r)\big]^{\top}+K_{b}(r-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r)\big]+\int^{T}_{r}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r)\big]^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\int^{r}_{t}K_{b}(s-t)^{\top}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s,\\
&\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}]=K_{\sigma}(T-t)Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r,t)^{\top}+K_{\sigma}(r-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r,t)+\int^{T}_{r}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r,t)^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}s\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\int^{r}_{t}K_{\sigma}(s-t)^{\top}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s,t)\,\mathrm{d}s.\end{split}

(B.23)

From these expressions, together with the fourth equation in the BSVIE system (
B.4
), we see that (
B.22
) holds. Hence,
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
is the solution of the BSEE (
B.12
).

(2-B): From
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
,
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
and
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
to
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4})
.
Assume that
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
is the solution of the BSEE (
4.32
), that
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
is the solution of the BSEE (
B.10
), and that
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
is the solution of the BSEE (
B.12
) for each
r\in[0,T]
such that
(\omega,t,\theta,r)\mapsto\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[0,r]}(t),\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}(\theta)\mbox{\rm{1}}\hskip-2.5pt\mbox{\rm{l}}_{[0,r]}(t)\in\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
are progressively measurable. Define
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4})
by (
B.13
), together with the martingale representations (
B.5
) and (
B.6
). By the same argument as in (1-B) above, we see that
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},Q^{V}_{2})
solves the first and second equations in the BSVIE system (
B.4
). It remains to show that
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4})
solves the third and fourth equations in (
B.4
).

From the BSEE (
B.10
) for
(\mathcal{P},\mathcal{Q})
, using Itô’s formula, we see that, for each
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{P}_{t}(\theta)

\displaystyle=-e^{-\theta(T-t)}\hat{h}_{xx}+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathcal{P}_{s}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathcal{Q}_{s}]\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s-\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathcal{Q}_{s}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},

\displaystyle\hskip 199.16928ptt\in[0,T].

Similarly, from the BSEE (
B.12
) for
(\mathscr{P}^{r},\mathscr{Q}^{r})
, we have

\begin{split}\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta)&=e^{-\theta(r-t)}\Big\{\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{r}],\hat{b}_{xx}(r)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{r}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(r)\big\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(r)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(r)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{r}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(r)\\
&\hskip 85.35826pt+\mu[\hat{P}_{r}(\theta,\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(r)+\mu[\hat{Q}_{r}(\theta,\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(r)\Big\}\\
&\hskip 28.45274pt+\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\Big\{\hat{b}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathscr{P}^{r}_{s}]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{s}]\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s-\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{s}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},\\
&\hskip 199.16928pt0\leq t\leq r\leq T.\end{split}

(B.24)

Define
\overline{P},\overline{Q}:\Omega\times[0,T]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}\to\mathbb{R}^{n\times n}
by

\displaystyle\overline{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}):=e^{-\theta_{2}(T-t)}\mathcal{P}_{t}(\theta_{1})+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(r-t)}\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta_{1})\,\mathrm{d}r,

\displaystyle\overline{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2}):=e^{-\theta_{2}(T-t)}\mathcal{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1})+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(r-t)}\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{t}(\theta_{1})\,\mathrm{d}r.

Then, the stochastic Fubini theorem yields that, for each
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
,

\displaystyle\overline{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})

\displaystyle=-e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(T-t)}\hat{h}_{xx}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(s-t)}\Big\{\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{s}],\hat{b}_{xx}(s)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{s}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(s)\big\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(s)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{s}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)+\mu[\hat{P}_{s}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(s)+\mu[\hat{Q}_{s}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt+\hat{b}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\overline{P}_{s}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\overline{Q}_{s}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt-\int^{T}_{t}e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(s-t)}\overline{Q}_{s}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},\ \ t\in[0,T].

From this, we see that
(\overline{P},\overline{Q})
and
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
solve the same BSEE (
4.32
). Thanks to the uniqueness of the solution, we see that
\overline{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
and
\overline{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\hat{Q}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})
for
\mu^{\otimes 2}
-a.e.
(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\in\mathbb{R}_{+}^{2}
a.s. for a.e.
t\in[0,T]
. In particular, we have

\displaystyle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{r}(\cdot,\theta)]=\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\overline{P}_{r}(\cdot,\theta)]=e^{-\theta(T-r)}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathcal{P}_{r}]+\int^{T}_{r}e^{-\theta(s-r)}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\mathscr{P}^{s}_{r}]\mathrm{d}s,

\displaystyle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{r}(\cdot,\theta)]=\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\overline{Q}_{r}(\cdot,\theta)]=e^{-\theta(T-r)}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathcal{Q}_{r}]+\int^{T}_{r}e^{-\theta(s-r)}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\mathscr{Q}^{s}_{r}]\mathrm{d}s,

for
\mu
-a.e.
\theta\in\mathbb{R}_{+}
a.s. for a.e.
r\in[0,T]
. From these relations, together with the definitions (
B.13
) of
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}
and
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}
, we get the equalities in form of (
B.18
) and (
B.19
).

Inserting (
B.19
) and the definitions (
B.13
) of
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}
and
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}
to (
B.24
), we get

\displaystyle\mathscr{P}^{r}_{t}(\theta)+\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathscr{Q}^{r}_{s}(\theta)\,\mathrm{d}W_{s}

\displaystyle=e^{-\theta(T-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r)^{\top}+e^{-\theta(r-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r)+\int^{T}_{r}e^{-\theta(s-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r)^{\top}\mathrm{d}s+\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle=e^{-\theta(T-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r)\big]^{\top}+e^{-\theta(r-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r)\big]+\int^{T}_{r}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(s,r)\big]^{\top}\mathrm{d}s+\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta(s-t)}\Big\{e^{-\theta(T-s)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(r,s)^{\top}+e^{-\theta(r-s)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(r,s)

\displaystyle\hskip 113.81102pt+\int^{T}_{r}e^{-\theta(\tau-s)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(\tau,r,s)^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}\tau+\int^{r}_{s}e^{-\theta(\tau-s)}Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,\tau,s)\,\mathrm{d}\tau\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}W_{s},

where we used the martingale representation (
B.5
) and the stochastic Fubini theorem in the second equality. From this, we get the equalities in form of (
B.11
). Hence, as before, thanks to the representation formulas (
4.1
) for the kernels
K_{b}
and
K_{\sigma}
, using Fubini’s theorem, we get (
B.23
). Inserting the expressions (
B.23
) to the definition of
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}
in (
B.13
), we see that the fourth equation in (
B.4
) holds.

Furthermore, by the BSEE (
4.32
) for
(\hat{P},\hat{Q})
and the definitions (
B.13
) of
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}
and
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}
, together with the formulas (
B.18
) and (
B.19
), we have

\displaystyle\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})=\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[\hat{P}_{t}(\theta_{1},\theta_{2})\big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{E}_{t}\Big[-e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(T-t)}\hat{h}_{xx}\vphantom{\int^{T}_{t}}

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(s-t)}\Big\{\big\langle\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{p}_{s}],\hat{b}_{xx}(s)\big\rangle+\big\langle\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{q}_{s}],\hat{\sigma}_{xx}(s)\big\rangle-\hat{f}_{xx}(s)+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu^{\otimes 2}[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{P}_{s}M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)\vphantom{\int^{T}_{t}}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+\hat{b}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu[M_{b}^{\top}\hat{P}_{s}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)^{\top}\mu[M_{\sigma}^{\top}\hat{Q}_{s}(\cdot,\theta_{2})]+\mu[\hat{P}_{s}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{b}]\hat{b}_{x}(s)+\mu[\hat{Q}_{s}(\theta_{1},\cdot)M_{\sigma}]\hat{\sigma}_{x}(s)\Big\}\,\mathrm{d}s\Big]

\displaystyle=e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(T-t)}P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1}(t)+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-(\theta_{1}+\theta_{2})(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}(s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+e^{-\theta_{2}(T-t)}\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{1}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(r-t)}\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta_{1}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}r

\displaystyle\hskip 14.22636pt+e^{-\theta_{1}(T-t)}\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2}(s)\big]^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}s+\int^{T}_{t}e^{-\theta_{1}(r-t)}\int^{r}_{t}e^{-\theta_{2}(s-t)}\mathbb{E}_{t}\big[P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4}(r,s)\big]^{\top}\,\mathrm{d}s\,\mathrm{d}r.

Namely, we have the equality in form of (
B.9
) for
\hat{P}
. Hence, as before, thanks to the representation formula (
4.1
) for
K_{\sigma}
, using Fubini’s theorem, we get (
B.20
). Inserting the expressions (
B.20
) and (
B.16
) to the definition of
P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3}
in (
B.13
), we see that the third equation in (
B.4
) holds.

Consequently,
(P^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{1},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{2},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{3},P^{\mathrm{V}}_{4},Q^{\mathrm{V}}_{4})
is the adapted M-solution of the BSVIE system (
B.4
). This completes the proof.
∎
